{\mathcal{H}}_{\varepsilon}K^{+}_{j}
, and hence of
{\mathcal{I}}_{\Omega,\Omega}[\varepsilon]h^{+}_{j}
.

Lemma 6.7
.

Let
R\geq 2R_{0}
and
j\geq 1
.

(i) We have the following bounds for
K^{+}_{j}
in
{\mathsf{P}}\cap{\mathscr{B}}(R)
, with a constant
C
independent of
j
and
R
:

(6.20a)

\displaystyle\mathchoice{\|\hskip 0.6458pt\nabla K^{+}_{j}\hskip 0.6458pt\|_{\raise-1.80835pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle L^{2}({\mathsf{P}}\cap{\mathscr{B}}(R))$}}\,}{\|\nabla K^{+}_{j}\|_{L^{2}({\mathsf{P}}\cap{\mathscr{B}}(R))}}{\|\nabla K^{+}_{j}\|_{L^{2}({\mathsf{P}}\cap{\mathscr{B}}(R))}}{\|\nabla K^{+}_{j}\|_{L^{2}({\mathsf{P}}\cap{\mathscr{B}}(R))}}

\displaystyle\leq C(\lambda^{+}_{j}+1)^{1/2}\,R^{\lambda^{+}_{j}-1+\frac{n}{2}},

(6.20b)

\displaystyle\mathchoice{\|\hskip 0.6458ptK^{+}_{j}\hskip 0.6458pt\|_{\raise-1.80835pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle L^{2}({\mathsf{P}}\cap{\mathscr{B}}(R))$}}\,}{\|K^{+}_{j}\|_{L^{2}({\mathsf{P}}\cap{\mathscr{B}}(R))}}{\|K^{+}_{j}\|_{L^{2}({\mathsf{P}}\cap{\mathscr{B}}(R))}}{\|K^{+}_{j}\|_{L^{2}({\mathsf{P}}\cap{\mathscr{B}}(R))}}

\displaystyle\leq C(\lambda^{+}_{j}+1)^{1/2}\,R^{\lambda^{+}_{j}+\frac{n}{2}}.

(ii) Let
\varepsilon\in(0,\frac{\varepsilon_{0}}{4})
and
\rho\in[2\varepsilon R_{0},r_{0}/2]
.
Then we have the following bound for
{\mathcal{H}}_{\varepsilon}K^{+}_{j}
in
\varepsilon{\mathsf{P}}\cap{\mathscr{B}}(\rho)
, with a constant
C
independent of
\varepsilon
,
j
and
\rho
:

(6.21)

\mathchoice{\|\hskip 0.6458pt{\mathcal{H}}_{\varepsilon}K^{+}_{j}\hskip 0.6458pt\|_{\raise-1.80835pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle H^{1}(\varepsilon{\mathsf{P}}\cap{\mathscr{B}}(\rho))$}}\,}{\|{\mathcal{H}}_{\varepsilon}K^{+}_{j}\|_{H^{1}(\varepsilon{\mathsf{P}}\cap{\mathscr{B}}(\rho))}}{\|{\mathcal{H}}_{\varepsilon}K^{+}_{j}\|_{H^{1}(\varepsilon{\mathsf{P}}\cap{\mathscr{B}}(\rho))}}{\|{\mathcal{H}}_{\varepsilon}K^{+}_{j}\|_{H^{1}(\varepsilon{\mathsf{P}}\cap{\mathscr{B}}(\rho))}}\leq C(\lambda^{+}_{j}+1)^{1/2}\,\left(\frac{\rho}{\varepsilon}\right)^{\lambda^{+}_{j}}.

Proof.

In what follows, we denote by the same letter
C
some constants that are independent of
j
and
R
, but may depend on
\Phi
,
R_{0}
, and
{\mathsf{P}}
, and may vary across different inequalities.

(i)
We consider separately the two terms of
K^{+}_{j}
, namely
\Phi h^{+}_{j}
and
Y^{+}_{j}
.

\blacktriangleright\>
Concerning the term
\Phi h^{+}_{j}
, we observe that:

\mathchoice{\|\hskip 0.6458pt\Phi h^{+}_{j}\hskip 0.6458pt\|_{\raise-1.80835pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle L^{2}({\mathsf{P}}\cap{\mathscr{B}}(R))$}}\,}{\|\Phi h^{+}_{j}\|_{L^{2}({\mathsf{P}}\cap{\mathscr{B}}(R))}}{\|\Phi h^{+}_{j}\|_{L^{2}({\mathsf{P}}\cap{\mathscr{B}}(R))}}{\|\Phi h^{+}_{j}\|_{L^{2}({\mathsf{P}}\cap{\mathscr{B}}(R))}}\leq C\mathchoice{\|\hskip 0.6458pth^{+}_{j}\hskip 0.6458pt\|_{\raise-1.80835pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle L^{2}(\Gamma\cap{\mathscr{B}}(R))$}}\,}{\|h^{+}_{j}\|_{L^{2}(\Gamma\cap{\mathscr{B}}(R))}}{\|h^{+}_{j}\|_{L^{2}(\Gamma\cap{\mathscr{B}}(R))}}{\|h^{+}_{j}\|_{L^{2}(\Gamma\cap{\mathscr{B}}(R))}}\,.

An explicit calculation yields

\mathchoice{\|\hskip 0.6458pth^{+}_{j}\hskip 0.6458pt\|_{\raise-1.80835pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle L^{2}(\Gamma\cap{\mathscr{B}}(R))$}}^{2}}{\|h^{+}_{j}\|_{L^{2}(\Gamma\cap{\mathscr{B}}(R))}^{2}}{\|h^{+}_{j}\|_{L^{2}(\Gamma\cap{\mathscr{B}}(R))}^{2}}{\|h^{+}_{j}\|_{L^{2}(\Gamma\cap{\mathscr{B}}(R))}^{2}}=\frac{1}{2\lambda^{+}_{j}+n}\,R^{2\lambda^{+}_{j}+n}\;(\;\leq R^{2\lambda^{+}_{j}+n})\,.

Hence,

(6.22)

\mathchoice{\|\hskip 0.6458pt\Phi h^{+}_{j}\hskip 0.6458pt\|_{\raise-1.80835pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle L^{2}({\mathsf{P}}\cap{\mathscr{B}}(R))$}}\,}{\|\Phi h^{+}_{j}\|_{L^{2}({\mathsf{P}}\cap{\mathscr{B}}(R))}}{\|\Phi h^{+}_{j}\|_{L^{2}({\mathsf{P}}\cap{\mathscr{B}}(R))}}{\|\Phi h^{+}_{j}\|_{L^{2}({\mathsf{P}}\cap{\mathscr{B}}(R))}}\leq C\,R^{\lambda^{+}_{j}+\frac{n}{2}}.

Similarly,

\mathchoice{\|\hskip 0.6458pt\nabla(\Phi h^{+}_{j})\hskip 0.6458pt\|_{\raise-1.80835pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle L^{2}({\mathsf{P}}\cap{\mathscr{B}}(R))$}}\,}{\|\nabla(\Phi h^{+}_{j})\|_{L^{2}({\mathsf{P}}\cap{\mathscr{B}}(R))}}{\|\nabla(\Phi h^{+}_{j})\|_{L^{2}({\mathsf{P}}\cap{\mathscr{B}}(R))}}{\|\nabla(\Phi h^{+}_{j})\|_{L^{2}({\mathsf{P}}\cap{\mathscr{B}}(R))}}\leq C\big(\mathchoice{\|\hskip 0.6458pt\nabla h^{+}_{j}\hskip 0.6458pt\|_{\raise-1.80835pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle L^{2}(\Gamma\cap{\mathscr{B}}(R))$}}\,}{\|\nabla h^{+}_{j}\|_{L^{2}(\Gamma\cap{\mathscr{B}}(R))}}{\|\nabla h^{+}_{j}\|_{L^{2}(\Gamma\cap{\mathscr{B}}(R))}}{\|\nabla h^{+}_{j}\|_{L^{2}(\Gamma\cap{\mathscr{B}}(R))}}+\mathchoice{\|\hskip 0.6458pth^{+}_{j}\hskip 0.6458pt\|_{\raise-1.80835pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle L^{2}(\Gamma\cap{\mathscr{A}}(R_{0},2R_{0}))$}}\,}{\|h^{+}_{j}\|_{L^{2}(\Gamma\cap{\mathscr{A}}(R_{0},2R_{0}))}}{\|h^{+}_{j}\|_{L^{2}(\Gamma\cap{\mathscr{A}}(R_{0},2R_{0}))}}{\|h^{+}_{j}\|_{L^{2}(\Gamma\cap{\mathscr{A}}(R_{0},2R_{0}))}}\big).

Hence, using (
4.27a
) and the formula for
\mathchoice{\|\hskip 0.6458pth^{+}_{j}\hskip 0.6458pt\|_{\raise-1.80835pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle L^{2}(\Gamma\cap{\mathscr{B}}(2R_{0}))$}}^{2}}{\|h^{+}_{j}\|_{L^{2}(\Gamma\cap{\mathscr{B}}(2R_{0}))}^{2}}{\|h^{+}_{j}\|_{L^{2}(\Gamma\cap{\mathscr{B}}(2R_{0}))}^{2}}{\|h^{+}_{j}\|_{L^{2}(\Gamma\cap{\mathscr{B}}(2R_{0}))}^{2}}
,

\mathchoice{\|\hskip 0.6458pt\nabla(\Phi h^{+}_{j})\hskip 0.6458pt\|_{\raise-1.80835pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle L^{2}({\mathsf{P}}\cap{\mathscr{B}}(R))$}}\,}{\|\nabla(\Phi h^{+}_{j})\|_{L^{2}({\mathsf{P}}\cap{\mathscr{B}}(R))}}{\|\nabla(\Phi h^{+}_{j})\|_{L^{2}({\mathsf{P}}\cap{\mathscr{B}}(R))}}{\|\nabla(\Phi h^{+}_{j})\|_{L^{2}({\mathsf{P}}\cap{\mathscr{B}}(R))}}\leq C\left(\sqrt{\lambda^{+}_{j}}\,R^{\lambda^{+}_{j}-1+\frac{n}{2}}+(2R_{0})^{\lambda^{+}_{j}+\frac{n}{2}}\right)\,.

Since
R\geq 2R_{0}
, we can write
(2R_{0})^{\lambda^{+}_{j}+\frac{n}{2}}\leq 2R_{0}\,R^{\lambda^{+}_{j}-1+\frac{n}{2}}
and deduce from the previous inequality that

(6.23)

\mathchoice{\|\hskip 0.6458pt\nabla(\Phi h^{+}_{j})\hskip 0.6458pt\|_{\raise-1.80835pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle L^{2}({\mathsf{P}}\cap{\mathscr{B}}(R))$}}\,}{\|\nabla(\Phi h^{+}_{j})\|_{L^{2}({\mathsf{P}}\cap{\mathscr{B}}(R))}}{\|\nabla(\Phi h^{+}_{j})\|_{L^{2}({\mathsf{P}}\cap{\mathscr{B}}(R))}}{\|\nabla(\Phi h^{+}_{j})\|_{L^{2}({\mathsf{P}}\cap{\mathscr{B}}(R))}}\leq C(\lambda^{+}_{j}+1)^{1/2}\,R^{\lambda^{+}_{j}-1+\frac{n}{2}}.

\blacktriangleright\>
Concerning the term
Y^{+}_{j}
, set
C^{+}_{j}:=[\Delta_{X},\Phi]h^{+}_{j}
so that
{\mathcal{M}}_{{\mathsf{P}},{\mathsf{P}}}^{-1}C^{+}_{j}=Y^{+}_{j}
. Relying on (
3.3b
), we start from the resolvent estimate:

\mathchoice{\|\hskip 0.6458ptY^{+}_{j}\hskip 0.6458pt\|_{\raise-1.80835pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle H^{1}_{\mathsf{w}}({\mathsf{P}})$}}\,}{\|Y^{+}_{j}\|_{H^{1}_{\mathsf{w}}({\mathsf{P}})}}{\|Y^{+}_{j}\|_{H^{1}_{\mathsf{w}}({\mathsf{P}})}}{\|Y^{+}_{j}\|_{H^{1}_{\mathsf{w}}({\mathsf{P}})}}\leq\mathchoice{\|\hskip 0.6458ptY^{+}_{j}\hskip 0.6458pt\|_{\raise-1.80835pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathfrak{E}_{\mathsf{P}}$}}\,}{\|Y^{+}_{j}\|_{\mathfrak{E}_{\mathsf{P}}}}{\|Y^{+}_{j}\|_{\mathfrak{E}_{\mathsf{P}}}}{\|Y^{+}_{j}\|_{\mathfrak{E}_{\mathsf{P}}}}=\mathchoice{\|\hskip 0.6458pt{\mathcal{M}}_{{\mathsf{P}},{\mathsf{P}}}^{-1}C^{+}_{j}\hskip 0.6458pt\|_{\raise-1.80835pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathfrak{E}_{\mathsf{P}}$}}\,}{\|{\mathcal{M}}_{{\mathsf{P}},{\mathsf{P}}}^{-1}C^{+}_{j}\|_{\mathfrak{E}_{\mathsf{P}}}}{\|{\mathcal{M}}_{{\mathsf{P}},{\mathsf{P}}}^{-1}C^{+}_{j}\|_{\mathfrak{E}_{\mathsf{P}}}}{\|{\mathcal{M}}_{{\mathsf{P}},{\mathsf{P}}}^{-1}C^{+}_{j}\|_{\mathfrak{E}_{\mathsf{P}}}}\leq C\mathchoice{\|\hskip 0.6458ptC^{+}_{j}\hskip 0.6458pt\|_{\raise-1.80835pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathfrak{F}_{\mathsf{P}}$}}\,}{\|C^{+}_{j}\|_{\mathfrak{F}_{\mathsf{P}}}}{\|C^{+}_{j}\|_{\mathfrak{F}_{\mathsf{P}}}}{\|C^{+}_{j}\|_{\mathfrak{F}_{\mathsf{P}}}}=C\mathchoice{\|\hskip 0.6458ptC^{+}_{j}\hskip 0.6458pt\|_{\raise-1.80835pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle L^{2}({\mathsf{P}})$}}\,}{\|C^{+}_{j}\|_{L^{2}({\mathsf{P}})}}{\|C^{+}_{j}\|_{L^{2}({\mathsf{P}})}}{\|C^{+}_{j}\|_{L^{2}({\mathsf{P}})}}\,.

But, since
C^{+}_{j}=[\Delta_{X},\Phi]h^{+}_{j}
, we have:

\displaystyle\mathchoice{\|\hskip 0.6458ptC^{+}_{j}\hskip 0.6458pt\|_{\raise-1.80835pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle L^{2}({\mathsf{P}})$}}\,}{\|C^{+}_{j}\|_{L^{2}({\mathsf{P}})}}{\|C^{+}_{j}\|_{L^{2}({\mathsf{P}})}}{\|C^{+}_{j}\|_{L^{2}({\mathsf{P}})}}

\displaystyle\leq C\mathchoice{\|\hskip 0.6458pth^{+}_{j}\hskip 0.6458pt\|_{\raise-1.80835pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle H^{1}(\Gamma\cap{\mathscr{A}}(R_{0},2R_{0}))$}}\,}{\|h^{+}_{j}\|_{H^{1}(\Gamma\cap{\mathscr{A}}(R_{0},2R_{0}))}}{\|h^{+}_{j}\|_{H^{1}(\Gamma\cap{\mathscr{A}}(R_{0},2R_{0}))}}{\|h^{+}_{j}\|_{H^{1}(\Gamma\cap{\mathscr{A}}(R_{0},2R_{0}))}}

\displaystyle\leq C\left(\sqrt{\lambda^{+}_{j}}\,(2R_{0})^{\lambda^{+}_{j}-1+\frac{n}{2}}+(2R_{0})^{\lambda^{+}_{j}+\frac{n}{2}}\right)

\displaystyle\leq C(\lambda^{+}_{j}+1)^{1/2}\,(2R_{0})^{\lambda^{+}_{j}-1+\frac{n}{2}}.

Observing that for any element
w
of
H^{1}_{\mathsf{w}}({\mathsf{P}})
there holds

\mathchoice{\|\hskip 0.6458ptw\hskip 0.6458pt\|_{\raise-1.80835pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle H^{1}_{\mathsf{w}}({\mathsf{P}})$}}^{2}}{\|w\|_{H^{1}_{\mathsf{w}}({\mathsf{P}})}^{2}}{\|w\|_{H^{1}_{\mathsf{w}}({\mathsf{P}})}^{2}}{\|w\|_{H^{1}_{\mathsf{w}}({\mathsf{P}})}^{2}}\geq\mathchoice{\|\hskip 0.6458pt\nabla w\hskip 0.6458pt\|_{\raise-1.80835pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle L^{2}({\mathsf{P}}\cap{\mathscr{B}}(R))$}}^{2}}{\|\nabla w\|_{L^{2}({\mathsf{P}}\cap{\mathscr{B}}(R))}^{2}}{\|\nabla w\|_{L^{2}({\mathsf{P}}\cap{\mathscr{B}}(R))}^{2}}{\|\nabla w\|_{L^{2}({\mathsf{P}}\cap{\mathscr{B}}(R))}^{2}}+\frac{1}{R^{2}+1}\mathchoice{\|\hskip 0.6458ptw\hskip 0.6458pt\|_{\raise-1.80835pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle L^{2}({\mathsf{P}}\cap{\mathscr{B}}(R))$}}^{2}}{\|w\|_{L^{2}({\mathsf{P}}\cap{\mathscr{B}}(R))}^{2}}{\|w\|_{L^{2}({\mathsf{P}}\cap{\mathscr{B}}(R))}^{2}}{\|w\|_{L^{2}({\mathsf{P}}\cap{\mathscr{B}}(R))}^{2}}

we deduce for
Y^{+}_{j}
:

(6.24)

\mathchoice{\|\hskip 0.6458pt\nabla Y^{+}_{j}\hskip 0.6458pt\|_{\raise-1.80835pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle L^{2}({\mathsf{P}}\cap{\mathscr{B}}(R))$}}\,}{\|\nabla Y^{+}_{j}\|_{L^{2}({\mathsf{P}}\cap{\mathscr{B}}(R))}}{\|\nabla Y^{+}_{j}\|_{L^{2}({\mathsf{P}}\cap{\mathscr{B}}(R))}}{\|\nabla Y^{+}_{j}\|_{L^{2}({\mathsf{P}}\cap{\mathscr{B}}(R))}}+\frac{1}{R+1}\mathchoice{\|\hskip 0.6458ptY^{+}_{j}\hskip 0.6458pt\|_{\raise-1.80835pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle L^{2}({\mathsf{P}}\cap{\mathscr{B}}(R))$}}\,}{\|Y^{+}_{j}\|_{L^{2}({\mathsf{P}}\cap{\mathscr{B}}(R))}}{\|Y^{+}_{j}\|_{L^{2}({\mathsf{P}}\cap{\mathscr{B}}(R))}}{\|Y^{+}_{j}\|_{L^{2}({\mathsf{P}}\cap{\mathscr{B}}(R))}}\leq C(\lambda^{+}_{j}+1)^{1/2}\,(2R_{0})^{\lambda^{+}_{j}-1+\frac{n}{2}}.

\blacktriangleright\>
Putting (
6.22
), (
6.23
), and (
6.24
) together, and using the fact that
R\geq 2R_{0}
, we obtain the bounds (
6.20a
) and (
6.20b
).

(ii)
To obtain the bound (
6.21
) for
K^{+}_{j}
as a function of the rapid variable
{x}/{\varepsilon}
, it suffices to use estimates (
6.20a
) and (
6.20b
) with
R={\rho}/{\varepsilon}
, which is greater than or equal to
2R_{0}
due to the assumption that
\rho\geq 2R_{0}\varepsilon
, and then use the change of variables
x=\varepsilon X
in integrals and derivatives.
∎

From estimate (
6.21
)
we readily deduce a bound for
{\mathcal{I}}_{\Omega,\Omega}[\varepsilon]h^{+}_{j}=\varepsilon^{\lambda^{+}_{j}}{\mathcal{H}}_{\varepsilon}K^{+}_{j}
, which leads to a representation of the inner expansion operator
{\mathcal{I}}_{\Omega,\Omega}
as a normally convergent series:

Proposition 6.8
.

Let
\mathbf{c}_{j}
be the trace operator defined in (
4.28
).
The inner expansion operator
{\mathcal{I}}_{\Omega,\Omega}[\varepsilon]
(
6.16
)–(
6.19
) satisfies

(6.25)

\forall u\in\mathfrak{E}_{\Omega},\quad{\mathcal{I}}_{\Omega,\Omega}[\varepsilon]u=\sum_{j\geq 1}\varepsilon^{\lambda^{+}_{j}}\mathbf{c}_{j}(u)\;{\mathcal{H}}_{\varepsilon}K^{+}_{j},

with normal convergence in
H^{1}
-norm in the region
\Omega_{\varepsilon}\cap{\mathscr{B}}({r_{0}}/{2})
in the following sense:
For any
\varepsilon_{1}\in(0,\frac{\varepsilon_{0}}{4})
, for any
r_{1}\in(0,\frac{r_{0}}{2})
, there exists a constant
C
such that

(6.26)

\forall\varepsilon\in(0,\varepsilon_{1}],\quad\forall u\in\mathfrak{E}_{\Omega},\quad\sum_{j\geq 1}\varepsilon^{\lambda^{+}_{j}}|\mathbf{c}_{j}(u)|\;\mathchoice{\|\hskip 0.6458pt{\mathcal{H}}_{\varepsilon}K^{+}_{j}\hskip 0.6458pt\|_{\raise-1.80835pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle H^{1}(\Omega_{\varepsilon}\cap{\mathscr{B}}(r_{1}))$}}\,}{\|{\mathcal{H}}_{\varepsilon}K^{+}_{j}\|_{H^{1}(\Omega_{\varepsilon}\cap{\mathscr{B}}(r_{1}))}}{\|{\mathcal{H}}_{\varepsilon}K^{+}_{j}\|_{H^{1}(\Omega_{\varepsilon}\cap{\mathscr{B}}(r_{1}))}}{\|{\mathcal{H}}_{\varepsilon}K^{+}_{j}\|_{H^{1}(\Omega_{\varepsilon}\cap{\mathscr{B}}(r_{1}))}}\leq C\mathchoice{\|\hskip 0.6458ptu\hskip 0.6458pt\|_{\raise-1.80835pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathfrak{E}_{\Omega}$}}\,}{\|u\|_{\mathfrak{E}_{\Omega}}}{\|u\|_{\mathfrak{E}_{\Omega}}}{\|u\|_{\mathfrak{E}_{\Omega}}}.

Proof.

By Theorem
4.9

a)
, the harmonic function
u
can be expanded in
H^{1}_{0}(\Gamma\cap\overline{{\mathscr{B}}(r_{0}/2)})
as a series of the homogeneous harmonic functions
h^{+}_{j}
, as follows:

u=\sum_{j\geq 1}\mathbf{c}_{j}(u)h^{+}_{j}.

On one hand, using the homogeneity of the
h^{+}_{j}
, we obtain

({\mathcal{H}}_{1/\varepsilon}u)(X)=\sum_{j\geq 1}\mathbf{c}_{j}(u)h^{+}_{j}(\varepsilon X)=\sum_{j\geq 1}\varepsilon^{\lambda^{+}_{j}}\mathbf{c}_{j}(u)h^{+}_{j}(X).

On the other hand, we can use Theorem
4.11
to write, for
\varepsilon\in\left(0,\frac{\varepsilon_{0}}{4}\right)
,

{\mathcal{M}}_{{\mathsf{P}},\Omega}[\varepsilon]u=\sum_{j\geq 1}\varepsilon^{\lambda^{+}_{j}}\mathbf{c}_{j}(u)[\Delta_{X},\Phi]h^{+}_{j}.

This leads immediately to the expansion (
6.25
) of
{\mathcal{I}}_{\Omega,\Omega}[\varepsilon]u
:

\displaystyle{\mathcal{I}}_{\Omega,\Omega}[\varepsilon]u

\displaystyle=\sum_{j\geq 1}\varepsilon^{\lambda^{+}_{j}}\mathbf{c}_{j}(u)\;{\mathcal{H}}_{\varepsilon}\Big(\Phi h^{+}_{j}-{\mathcal{M}}_{{\mathsf{P}},{\mathsf{P}}}^{-1}[\Delta_{X},\Phi]h^{+}_{j}\Big)

\displaystyle=\sum_{j\geq 1}\varepsilon^{\lambda^{+}_{j}}\mathbf{c}_{j}(u)\;{\mathcal{H}}_{\varepsilon}K^{+}_{j}.

Let us check the normal convergence.
Using (
4.29
) (with the specific choice
\rho=r_{0}/2
), we deduce that

|\mathbf{c}_{j}(u)|\leq(\lambda^{+}_{j})^{-1/2}\left(\frac{r_{0}}{2}\right)^{-\lambda^{+}_{j}-1+n/2}\mathchoice{\|\hskip 0.6458pt\nabla u\hskip 0.6458pt\|_{\raise-1.80835pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle L^{2}(\Gamma\cap{\mathscr{B}}(r_{0}/2))$}}\,}{\|\nabla u\|_{L^{2}(\Gamma\cap{\mathscr{B}}(r_{0}/2))}}{\|\nabla u\|_{L^{2}(\Gamma\cap{\mathscr{B}}(r_{0}/2))}}{\|\nabla u\|_{L^{2}(\Gamma\cap{\mathscr{B}}(r_{0}/2))}}\,.

Let
\varepsilon\in(0,\frac{\varepsilon_{0}}{4})
.
Combining the inequality above with (
6.21
) for
\rho\in[2\varepsilon R_{0},r_{0}/2)
leads to

\displaystyle\sum_{j\geq 1}\varepsilon^{\lambda^{+}_{j}}|\mathbf{c}_{j}(u)|

\displaystyle\mathchoice{\|\hskip 0.6458pt{\mathcal{H}}_{\varepsilon}K^{+}_{j}\hskip 0.6458pt\|_{\raise-1.80835pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle H^{1}(\varepsilon{\mathsf{P}}\cap{\mathscr{B}}(\rho))$}}\,}{\|{\mathcal{H}}_{\varepsilon}K^{+}_{j}\|_{H^{1}(\varepsilon{\mathsf{P}}\cap{\mathscr{B}}(\rho))}}{\|{\mathcal{H}}_{\varepsilon}K^{+}_{j}\|_{H^{1}(\varepsilon{\mathsf{P}}\cap{\mathscr{B}}(\rho))}}{\|{\mathcal{H}}_{\varepsilon}K^{+}_{j}\|_{H^{1}(\varepsilon{\mathsf{P}}\cap{\mathscr{B}}(\rho))}}

\displaystyle\leq\left(\frac{r_{0}}{2}\right)^{-1+n/2}\mathchoice{\|\hskip 0.6458pt\nabla u\hskip 0.6458pt\|_{\raise-1.80835pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle L^{2}(\Gamma\cap{\mathscr{B}}(r_{0}/2))$}}\,}{\|\nabla u\|_{L^{2}(\Gamma\cap{\mathscr{B}}(r_{0}/2))}}{\|\nabla u\|_{L^{2}(\Gamma\cap{\mathscr{B}}(r_{0}/2))}}{\|\nabla u\|_{L^{2}(\Gamma\cap{\mathscr{B}}(r_{0}/2))}}\sum_{j\geq 1}\varepsilon^{\lambda^{+}_{j}}\frac{(\lambda^{+}_{j}+1)^{1/2}}{(\lambda^{+}_{j})^{1/2}}\,\left(\frac{r_{0}}{2}\right)^{-\lambda^{+}_{j}}\left(\frac{\rho}{\varepsilon}\right)^{\lambda^{+}_{j}}

\displaystyle\leq C\mathchoice{\|\hskip 0.6458ptu\hskip 0.6458pt\|_{\raise-1.80835pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathfrak{E}_{\Omega}$}}\,}{\|u\|_{\mathfrak{E}_{\Omega}}}{\|u\|_{\mathfrak{E}_{\Omega}}}{\|u\|_{\mathfrak{E}_{\Omega}}}\sum_{j\geq 1}\left(\frac{2\rho}{r_{0}}\right)^{\lambda^{+}_{j}}.

As
\rho<r_{0}/2
, Lemma
4.14
yields the convergence of the latter series, from which we deduce inequality (
6.26
).
∎

Remark 6.9
.

From Proposition
6.8
, we see more clearly how a function
u
, harmonic near the vertex of
\Gamma
and with Dirichlet conditions on
\partial\Gamma
, is transformed by
{\mathcal{I}}_{\Omega,\Omega}[\varepsilon]
into another harmonic function with Dirichlet conditions on
\varepsilon\partial{\mathsf{P}}
through the formulas

u=\sum_{j\geq 1}\varepsilon^{\lambda^{+}_{j}}\mathbf{c}_{j}(u)\;{\mathcal{H}}_{\varepsilon}h^{+}_{j}\quad\mbox{and}\quad{\mathcal{I}}_{\Omega,\Omega}[\varepsilon]u=\sum_{j\geq 1}\varepsilon^{\lambda^{+}_{j}}\mathbf{c}_{j}(u)\;{\mathcal{H}}_{\varepsilon}K^{+}_{j}.

We are now ready to prove Theorem
6.10
, where we finally apply
{\mathcal{I}}_{\Omega,\Omega}[\varepsilon]
to the function
{\mathsf{u}}[\varepsilon]=\sum_{{\mathsf{e}}\in{\mathsf{E}}^{\infty}}\varepsilon^{{\mathsf{e}}}{\mathsf{u}}_{\mathsf{e}}
from Theorem
6.1
.

Theorem 6.10
.

Let
f\in\mathfrak{F}_{\Omega}
,
F\in\mathfrak{F}_{\mathsf{P}}
, and let
u_{\varepsilon}
be the solution of problem (
3.4
).
Under the assumptions of Theorem
6.1
, and with
K^{+}_{j}
being the canonical harmonic function introduced in Definition
6.4
a),
we have for all
0<\varepsilon<\varepsilon_{\star}
:

(6.27)

u_{\varepsilon}(x)=U_{0}\left(\frac{x}{\varepsilon}\right)+\sum_{j\geq 1}\sum_{{\mathsf{e}}\in{\mathsf{E}}^{\infty}}\varepsilon^{{\mathsf{e}}+\lambda^{+}_{j}}\mathbf{c}_{j}({\mathsf{u}}_{\mathsf{e}})\,K^{+}_{j}\left(\frac{x}{\varepsilon}\right)\,,\quad\mbox{a.e.}\;\;x\in\Omega_{\varepsilon}\cap{\mathscr{B}}(r_{0}/2)

where
{\mathsf{u}}_{\mathsf{e}}
are the coefficients in the series
\sum_{{\mathsf{e}}\in{\mathsf{E}}^{\infty}}\varepsilon^{{\mathsf{e}}}{\mathsf{u}}_{\mathsf{e}}={\mathsf{u}}[\varepsilon]
, which is the slow part in the global expansion (
6.2
). Moreover, for all
r_{1}\in(0,r_{0}/2)
and all
\varepsilon_{1}\in(0,\varepsilon_{\star})
the series (
6.27
) converges normally in
H^{1}(\Omega_{\varepsilon}\cap{\mathscr{B}}(r_{1}))
with the estimates

(6.28)

\sum_{j\geq 1}\sum_{{\mathsf{e}}\in{\mathsf{E}}^{\infty}}\varepsilon^{{\mathsf{e}}+\lambda^{+}_{j}}|\mathbf{c}_{j}({\mathsf{u}}_{\mathsf{e}})|\left\|K^{+}_{j}\left(\frac{\cdot}{\varepsilon}\right)\right\|_{H^{1}(\Omega_{\varepsilon}\cap{{\mathscr{B}}(r_{1})})}\leq C\left(\mathchoice{\|\hskip 0.6458ptf\hskip 0.6458pt\|_{\raise-1.80835pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathfrak{F}_{\Omega}$}}\,}{\|f\|_{\mathfrak{F}_{\Omega}}}{\|f\|_{\mathfrak{F}_{\Omega}}}{\|f\|_{\mathfrak{F}_{\Omega}}}+\mathchoice{\|\hskip 0.6458ptF\hskip 0.6458pt\|_{\raise-1.80835pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathfrak{F}_{\mathsf{P}}$}}\,}{\|F\|_{\mathfrak{F}_{\mathsf{P}}}}{\|F\|_{\mathfrak{F}_{\mathsf{P}}}}{\|F\|_{\mathfrak{F}_{\mathsf{P}}}}\right)\,,

with
C
a constant independent of
\varepsilon
,
f
, and
F
.

Proof.

Recall that from (
6.17
) we have

u_{\varepsilon}={\mathcal{H}}_{\varepsilon}U_{0}+{\mathcal{I}}_{\Omega,\Omega}[\varepsilon]{\mathsf{u}}[\varepsilon]
,
with the series
{\mathsf{u}}[\varepsilon]=\sum_{{\mathsf{e}}\in{\mathsf{E}}^{\infty}}\varepsilon^{{\mathsf{e}}}{\mathsf{u}}_{\mathsf{e}}
normally converging in
\mathfrak{E}_{\Omega}
. Then, the result of the Theorem follows by linearity from Proposition
6.8
. Also note that the series (
6.28
) is bounded by
C\sum_{{\mathsf{e}}\in{\mathsf{E}}^{\infty}}\varepsilon^{{\mathsf{e}}}\mathchoice{\|\hskip 0.6458pt{\mathsf{u}}_{\mathsf{e}}\hskip 0.6458pt\|_{\raise-1.80835pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathfrak{E}_{\Omega}$}}\,}{\|{\mathsf{u}}_{\mathsf{e}}\|_{\mathfrak{E}_{\Omega}}}{\|{\mathsf{u}}_{\mathsf{e}}\|_{\mathfrak{E}_{\Omega}}}{\|{\mathsf{u}}_{\mathsf{e}}\|_{\mathfrak{E}_{\Omega}}}
.
∎

Remark 6.11
.

Owing to the assumptions on the support of the right-hand side of equation (
3.4
), the inner expansion is rather simple:
u_{\varepsilon}-{\mathcal{H}}_{\varepsilon}U_{0}
appears as a sum in
j
of the canonical harmonic functions
K^{+}_{j}
with scalar coefficients
\sum_{{\mathsf{e}}\in{\mathsf{E}}^{\infty}}\varepsilon^{{\mathsf{e}}+\lambda^{+}_{j}}\mathbf{c}_{j}({\mathsf{u}}_{\mathsf{e}})
that are generalized power series. Analogous structures can be more or less easily recognized in other works. In
[
16
, Sec. 5]
, which considers the same problem in dimension
2
, a reorganization of the constitutive terms of the inner expansion would yield similar formulas. In
[
5
, Th. 4.7]
, which considers an angle with a coating of width
\varepsilon
, canonical piecewise harmonic profiles denoted there by
\mathfrak{K}^{\lambda}
are a building block of asymptotic expansions. In
[
22
]
, which addresses stochastic homogenization in a plane sector, this structure of canonical profile functions associated with standard singular functions (denoted there
\bar{\tau}_{n}
) appears clearly as early as eqs. (6) and (7) in the form of the sum
\bar{\tau}_{n}+\phi^{\mathfrak{C}}_{n}
with the corrector
\phi^{\mathfrak{C}}_{n}
. By contrast, the very broad generality of the impressive
[
32
, Chap. 4]
makes it difficult to identify constitutive structures inside asymptotic expansions.

6.4.
Outer expansion

The strategy to obtain the outer expansion, away from the vertex of the cone, is similar to the one used for the inner expansion. However, we adopt a different perspective. Instead of starting from (
6.5
), we begin with formula (
6.4
), with
x
in the region
\Omega_{\varepsilon}\cap{\mathscr{B}}^{\complement}(\varepsilon R_{0})=\Omega\cap{\mathscr{B}}^{\complement}(\varepsilon R_{0})
(where
{\mathcal{H}}_{\varepsilon}\Phi\equiv 1
):

(6.29)

u_{\varepsilon}(x)=u_{0}(x)+\varphi(x){\mathcal{H}}_{\varepsilon}{\mathsf{U}}[\varepsilon](x)-\left({\mathcal{M}}_{\Omega,\Omega}^{-1}{\mathcal{M}}_{\Omega,{\mathsf{P}}}[\varepsilon]{\mathsf{U}}[\varepsilon]\right)(x).

Recalling that
{\mathcal{M}}_{\Omega,{\mathsf{P}}}[\varepsilon]=[\Delta,\varphi]\circ{\mathcal{H}}_{\varepsilon}
, we introduce the outer expansion operator

(6.30)

{\mathcal{O}}_{\Omega,{\mathsf{P}}}[\varepsilon]=(\varphi\mathbb{I}-{\mathcal{M}}_{\Omega,\Omega}^{-1}[\Delta,\varphi])\circ{\mathcal{H}}_{\varepsilon},

so that

(6.31)

u_{\varepsilon}=u_{0}+{\mathcal{O}}_{\Omega,{\mathsf{P}}}[\varepsilon]{\mathsf{U}}[\varepsilon].

We wish to prove that
{\mathcal{O}}_{\Omega,{\mathsf{P}}}[\varepsilon]
expands into a converging series of bounded operators and apply it to the series expansion

{\mathsf{U}}[\varepsilon]=\sum_{{\mathsf{e}}\in{\mathsf{E}}^{\infty}}\varepsilon^{{\mathsf{e}}}{\mathsf{U}}_{\mathsf{e}}

from Theorem
6.1
to obtain an expansion of
u_{\varepsilon}
. Since
\Delta(\varphi\mathbb{I}-{\mathcal{M}}_{\Omega,\Omega}^{-1}[\Delta,\varphi])=\varphi\Delta
, for the canonical harmonic functions
K^{-}_{j}
and their correctors
Y^{-}_{j}
, we find:

Y^{-}_{j}={\mathcal{M}}_{\Omega,\Omega}^{-1}[\Delta,\varphi]h^{-}_{j}\quad\mbox{and}\quad K^{-}_{j}=\big(\varphi\mathbb{I}-{\mathcal{M}}_{\Omega,\Omega}^{-1}[\Delta,\varphi]\big)h^{-}_{j}

(see Notation
6.4
b)).

The proof of the following estimates for
K^{-}_{j}
follows the same lines as in Lemma
6.7
and is left to the reader. (It is even simpler here because the variational space is
H^{1}_{0}(\Omega)
, so we do not have to deal with weights.)

Lemma 6.12
.

Let
r\leq r_{0}/2
and
j\geq 1
be an integer.
We have the following bounds for
K^{-}_{j}
in
\Omega\cap{\mathscr{B}}^{\complement}(r)
, with a constant
C
independent of
j
and
R
:

(6.32)

\mathchoice{\|\hskip 0.6458ptK^{-}_{j}\hskip 0.6458pt\|_{\raise-1.80835pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle H^{1}(\Omega\cap{\mathscr{B}}^{\complement}(r))$}}\,}{\|K^{-}_{j}\|_{H^{1}(\Omega\cap{\mathscr{B}}^{\complement}(r))}}{\|K^{-}_{j}\|_{H^{1}(\Omega\cap{\mathscr{B}}^{\complement}(r))}}{\|K^{-}_{j}\|_{H^{1}(\Omega\cap{\mathscr{B}}^{\complement}(r))}}\leq C(|\lambda^{-}_{j}|+1)^{1/2}\,r^{\lambda^{-}_{j}-1+\frac{n}{2}}.

We are now ready to show that the outer expansion operator
{\mathcal{O}}_{\Omega,{\mathsf{P}}}[\varepsilon]
can be expressed as a convergent series of bounded operators.

Proposition 6.13
.

Let
\mathbf{B}_{j}
be the trace operator defined in (
4.31
).
The outer expansion operator
{\mathcal{O}}_{\Omega,{\mathsf{P}}}[\varepsilon]
(
6.30
) satisfies

(6.33)

\forall U\in\mathfrak{E}_{\mathsf{P}},\quad{\mathcal{O}}_{\Omega,{\mathsf{P}}}[\varepsilon]U=\sum_{j\geq 1}\varepsilon^{-\lambda^{-}_{j}}\mathbf{B}_{j}(U)\;K^{-}_{j},

with normal convergence in
H^{1}
-norm in the region
\Omega_{\varepsilon}\cap{\mathscr{B}}^{\complement}(2\varepsilon R_{0})
in the following sense:
For any
\varepsilon_{1}\in(0,\varepsilon_{0}/4)
and for any
R_{1}>2R_{0}
, there exists a constant
C
such that

(6.34)

\forall\varepsilon\in(0,\varepsilon_{1}],\quad\forall U\in\mathfrak{E}_{\mathsf{P}},\quad\sum_{j\geq 1}\varepsilon^{-\lambda^{-}_{j}}|\mathbf{B}_{j}(U)|\;\mathchoice{\|\hskip 0.6458ptK^{-}_{j}\hskip 0.6458pt\|_{\raise-1.80835pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle H^{1}(\Omega_{\varepsilon}\cap{\mathscr{B}}^{\complement}(\varepsilon R_{1}))$}}\,}{\|K^{-}_{j}\|_{H^{1}(\Omega_{\varepsilon}\cap{\mathscr{B}}^{\complement}(\varepsilon R_{1}))}}{\|K^{-}_{j}\|_{H^{1}(\Omega_{\varepsilon}\cap{\mathscr{B}}^{\complement}(\varepsilon R_{1}))}}{\|K^{-}_{j}\|_{H^{1}(\Omega_{\varepsilon}\cap{\mathscr{B}}^{\complement}(\varepsilon R_{1}))}}\leq C\mathchoice{\|\hskip 0.6458ptU\hskip 0.6458pt\|_{\raise-1.80835pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathfrak{E}_{\mathsf{P}}$}}\,}{\|U\|_{\mathfrak{E}_{\mathsf{P}}}}{\|U\|_{\mathfrak{E}_{\mathsf{P}}}}{\|U\|_{\mathfrak{E}_{\mathsf{P}}}}.

Proof.

Though similar to that of Proposition
6.8
, the proof is given for completeness:
By part
b)
of Theorem
4.9
, the harmonic function
U
can be expanded in
H^{1}_{{\mathsf{w}},0}(\Gamma\cap\overline{{\mathscr{B}}^{\complement}(2R_{0})})
as a series of the homogeneous harmonic functions
h^{-}_{j}
according to:

U=\sum_{j\geq 1}\mathbf{B}_{j}(U)h^{-}_{j}.

On one hand, using the homogeneity of the functions
h^{-}_{j}
, we obtain

({\mathcal{H}}_{\varepsilon}U)(x)=\sum_{j\geq 1}\mathbf{B}_{j}(u)h^{-}_{j}\left(\frac{x}{\varepsilon}\right)=\sum_{j\geq 1}\varepsilon^{-\lambda^{-}_{j}}\mathbf{B}_{j}(U)h^{-}_{j}(x).

On the other hand, we use Theorem
4.11
to write, for
\varepsilon\in(0,\frac{\varepsilon_{0}}{4})
,

{\mathcal{M}}_{\Omega,{\mathsf{P}}}[\varepsilon]U=\sum_{j\geq 1}\varepsilon^{-\lambda^{-}_{j}}\mathbf{B}_{j}(U)[\Delta_{x},\varphi]h^{-}_{j}.

This leads to the expansion (
6.33
) of
{\mathcal{O}}_{\Omega,{\mathsf{P}}}[\varepsilon]U
:

\displaystyle{\mathcal{O}}_{\Omega,{\mathsf{P}}}[\varepsilon]U

\displaystyle=\sum_{j\geq 1}\varepsilon^{-\lambda^{-}_{j}}\mathbf{B}_{j}(U)\;\Big(\varphi h^{-}_{j}-{\mathcal{M}}_{\Omega,\Omega}^{-1}[\Delta_{x},\varphi]h^{-}_{j}\Big)

\displaystyle=\sum_{j\geq 1}\varepsilon^{-\lambda^{-}_{j}}\mathbf{B}_{j}(U)\;K^{-}_{j}.

Let us check the normal convergence.
Using (
4.32
) (with
\rho=2R_{0}
) we deduce that

|\mathbf{B}_{j}(U)|\leq|\lambda^{-}_{j}|^{-1/2}(2R_{0})^{-\lambda^{-}_{j}-1+n/2}\mathchoice{\|\hskip 0.6458pt\nabla U\hskip 0.6458pt\|_{\raise-1.80835pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle L^{2}(\Gamma\cap{\mathscr{B}}^{\complement}(2R_{0}))$}}\,}{\|\nabla U\|_{L^{2}(\Gamma\cap{\mathscr{B}}^{\complement}(2R_{0}))}}{\|\nabla U\|_{L^{2}(\Gamma\cap{\mathscr{B}}^{\complement}(2R_{0}))}}{\|\nabla U\|_{L^{2}(\Gamma\cap{\mathscr{B}}^{\complement}(2R_{0}))}}.

Let
\varepsilon\in(0,\frac{\varepsilon_{0}}{4})
.
Combining the inequality above with (
6.32
) for
\rho\in(2\varepsilon R_{0},r_{0}/2]
leads to

\displaystyle\sum_{j\geq 1}\varepsilon^{-\lambda^{-}_{j}}|\mathbf{B}_{j}(U)|

\displaystyle\mathchoice{\|\hskip 0.6458ptK^{-}_{j}\hskip 0.6458pt\|_{\raise-1.80835pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle H^{1}(\Omega\cap{\mathscr{B}}^{\complement}(\rho))$}}\,}{\|K^{-}_{j}\|_{H^{1}(\Omega\cap{\mathscr{B}}^{\complement}(\rho))}}{\|K^{-}_{j}\|_{H^{1}(\Omega\cap{\mathscr{B}}^{\complement}(\rho))}}{\|K^{-}_{j}\|_{H^{1}(\Omega\cap{\mathscr{B}}^{\complement}(\rho))}}

\displaystyle\leq\mathchoice{\|\hskip 0.6458pt\nabla U\hskip 0.6458pt\|_{\raise-1.80835pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle L^{2}(\Gamma\cap{\mathscr{B}}^{\complement}(2R_{0}))$}}\,}{\|\nabla U\|_{L^{2}(\Gamma\cap{\mathscr{B}}^{\complement}(2R_{0}))}}{\|\nabla U\|_{L^{2}(\Gamma\cap{\mathscr{B}}^{\complement}(2R_{0}))}}{\|\nabla U\|_{L^{2}(\Gamma\cap{\mathscr{B}}^{\complement}(2R_{0}))}}\sum_{j\geq 1}\varepsilon^{-\lambda^{-}_{j}}|\lambda^{-}_{j}|^{-1/2}(2R_{0})^{-\lambda^{-}_{j}-1+n/2}\mathchoice{\|\hskip 0.6458ptK^{-}_{j}\hskip 0.6458pt\|_{\raise-1.80835pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle H^{1}(\Omega\cap{\mathscr{B}}^{\complement}(\rho))$}}\,}{\|K^{-}_{j}\|_{H^{1}(\Omega\cap{\mathscr{B}}^{\complement}(\rho))}}{\|K^{-}_{j}\|_{H^{1}(\Omega\cap{\mathscr{B}}^{\complement}(\rho))}}{\|K^{-}_{j}\|_{H^{1}(\Omega\cap{\mathscr{B}}^{\complement}(\rho))}}

\displaystyle\leq\mathchoice{\|\hskip 0.6458ptU\hskip 0.6458pt\|_{\raise-1.80835pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathfrak{E}_{\mathsf{P}}$}}\,}{\|U\|_{\mathfrak{E}_{\mathsf{P}}}}{\|U\|_{\mathfrak{E}_{\mathsf{P}}}}{\|U\|_{\mathfrak{E}_{\mathsf{P}}}}\sum_{j\geq 1}\varepsilon^{-\lambda^{-}_{j}}\frac{(|\lambda^{-}_{j}|+1)^{1/2}}{|\lambda^{-}_{j}|^{1/2}}\,(2R_{0})^{-\lambda^{-}_{j}-1+n/2}\rho^{\lambda^{-}_{j}-1+\frac{n}{2}}

\displaystyle\leq C(2R_{0}\rho)^{-1+\frac{n}{2}}\mathchoice{\|\hskip 0.6458ptU\hskip 0.6458pt\|_{\raise-1.80835pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathfrak{E}_{\mathsf{P}}$}}\,}{\|U\|_{\mathfrak{E}_{\mathsf{P}}}}{\|U\|_{\mathfrak{E}_{\mathsf{P}}}}{\|U\|_{\mathfrak{E}_{\mathsf{P}}}}\sum_{j\geq 1}\left(\frac{2R_{0}\varepsilon}{\rho}\right)^{-\lambda^{-}_{j}}.

Since
\rho>2\varepsilon R_{0}
, Lemma
4.14
yields the convergence of the latter series, from which we deduce inequality (
6.34
).
∎

Equality (
6.31
) can be written as
u_{\varepsilon}=u_{0}+{\mathcal{O}}_{\Omega,{\mathsf{P}}}\left(\sum_{{\mathsf{e}}\in{\mathsf{E}}^{\infty}}\varepsilon^{\mathsf{e}}{\mathsf{U}}_{\mathsf{e}}\right)
, which, thanks to the description of the operator
{\mathcal{O}}_{\Omega,{\mathsf{P}}}
as a normally converging series in Proposition
6.13
, yields the following outer expansion for
u_{\varepsilon}
:

Theorem 6.14
.

Let
f\in\mathfrak{F}_{\Omega}
,
F\in\mathfrak{F}_{\mathsf{P}}
, and let
u_{\varepsilon}
be the solution of problem (
3.4
).
Under the assumptions of Theorem
6.1
, and with
K^{-}_{j}
being the canonical harmonic function introduced in Definition
6.4
b),
we have for all
0<\varepsilon<\varepsilon_{\star}
:

(6.35)

u_{\varepsilon}(x)=u_{0}(x)+\sum_{j\geq 1}\sum_{{\mathsf{e}}\in{\mathsf{E}}^{\infty}}\varepsilon^{{\mathsf{e}}-\lambda^{-}_{j}}\mathbf{B}_{j}({\mathsf{U}}_{\mathsf{e}})\,K^{-}_{j}(x)\,,\quad\mbox{a.e.}\;\;x\in\Omega_{\varepsilon}\cap{\mathscr{B}}^{\complement}(2R_{0}\varepsilon)

where
\sum_{{\mathsf{e}}\in{\mathsf{E}}^{\infty}}\varepsilon^{{\mathsf{e}}}{\mathsf{U}}_{\mathsf{e}}={\mathsf{U}}[\varepsilon]
is the rapid part in the global expansion (
6.2
). Moreover, for all
R_{1}>2R_{0}
and all
\varepsilon_{1}\in(0,\varepsilon_{\star})
the series (
6.35
) converges normally in
H^{1}(\Omega_{\varepsilon}\cap{{\mathscr{B}}^{\complement}(\varepsilon R_{1})})
with the estimates

(6.36)

\sum_{j\geq 1}\sum_{{\mathsf{e}}\in{\mathsf{E}}^{\infty}}\varepsilon^{{\mathsf{e}}-\lambda^{-}_{j}}|\mathbf{B}_{j}({\mathsf{U}}_{\mathsf{e}})|\mathchoice{\|\hskip 0.6458ptK^{-}_{j}\hskip 0.6458pt\|_{\raise-1.80835pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle H^{1}(\Omega_{\varepsilon}\cap{{\mathscr{B}}^{\complement}(\varepsilon R_{1})})$}}\,}{\|K^{-}_{j}\|_{H^{1}(\Omega_{\varepsilon}\cap{{\mathscr{B}}^{\complement}(\varepsilon R_{1})})}}{\|K^{-}_{j}\|_{H^{1}(\Omega_{\varepsilon}\cap{{\mathscr{B}}^{\complement}(\varepsilon R_{1})})}}{\|K^{-}_{j}\|_{H^{1}(\Omega_{\varepsilon}\cap{{\mathscr{B}}^{\complement}(\varepsilon R_{1})})}}\leq C\left(\mathchoice{\|\hskip 0.6458ptf\hskip 0.6458pt\|_{\raise-1.80835pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathfrak{F}_{\Omega}$}}\,}{\|f\|_{\mathfrak{F}_{\Omega}}}{\|f\|_{\mathfrak{F}_{\Omega}}}{\|f\|_{\mathfrak{F}_{\Omega}}}+\mathchoice{\|\hskip 0.6458ptF\hskip 0.6458pt\|_{\raise-1.80835pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathfrak{F}_{\mathsf{P}}$}}\,}{\|F\|_{\mathfrak{F}_{\mathsf{P}}}}{\|F\|_{\mathfrak{F}_{\mathsf{P}}}}{\|F\|_{\mathfrak{F}_{\mathsf{P}}}}\right)\,,

with
C
a constant independent of
\varepsilon
,
f
, and
F
.

6.5.
Conclusion: Comparison of expansions

In conclusion of this paper, we now compare the three expansions (
6.2
) (global), (
6.27
) (inner), and (
6.35
) (outer). As a result, we will demonstrate that the coefficients of these expansions can be computed through a recursive procedure involving a finite number of numerical operations, starting from the solutions
u_{0}
and
U_{0}
of the limiting problems (
6.3
). While recursive procedures of this kind are familiar in the framework of the Matched Asymptotic Expansion method, our approach is different: we first establish a convergent global expansion and then derive the recursive algorithm. This method has the advantage of identifying interaction matrices that remain the same at each step of the procedure.

The comparison of the global, inner, and outer expansions is made possible by their convergence in the common annular transition region

{\mathscr{A}}_{\varepsilon}\cap\Gamma\quad\mbox{with}\quad{\mathscr{A}}_{\varepsilon}:={\mathscr{A}}(2R_{0}\varepsilon,r_{0}/2)\,.

Indeed, for any
\varepsilon\in(0,\varepsilon_{0}/4]
we have identities

{\mathscr{A}}_{\varepsilon}\cap\Gamma={\mathscr{A}}_{\varepsilon}\cap\Omega={\mathscr{A}}_{\varepsilon}\cap\varepsilon{\mathsf{P}}={\mathscr{A}}_{\varepsilon}\cap\Omega_{\varepsilon}\,,

and this transition region is the intersection between the domains of validity of inner and outer expansions.

Let
\varepsilon_{1}\in(0,\varepsilon_{\star})
and let
\rho\in(2R_{0}\varepsilon_{1},r_{0}/2)
(so that
\rho\in(2R_{0}\varepsilon,r_{0}/2)
for all
\varepsilon\in[0,\varepsilon_{1}]
). Choose
\varepsilon\in(0,\varepsilon_{1}]
and
let
x\in\rho\hat{\Gamma}
. Then
x
belongs to the transition region and moreover,
\varphi(x)=\Phi(\frac{x}{\varepsilon})=1
. With
f\in\mathfrak{F}_{\Omega}
and
F\in\mathfrak{F}_{\mathsf{P}}
, the global, inner, and outer expansions (
6.2
), (
6.27
), and (
6.35
) hold simultaneously at
x
. Accordingly, we have

\displaystyle u_{\varepsilon}(x)

\displaystyle=\sum_{{\mathsf{e}}\in{\mathsf{E}}^{\infty}}\varepsilon^{{\mathsf{e}}}{\mathsf{u}}_{\mathsf{e}}(x)+\sum_{{\mathsf{e}}\in{\mathsf{E}}^{\infty}}\varepsilon^{{\mathsf{e}}}{\mathsf{U}}_{\mathsf{e}}\left(\frac{x}{\varepsilon}\right)

\displaystyle=U_{0}\left(\frac{x}{\varepsilon}\right)+\sum_{j\geq 1}\sum_{{\mathsf{e}}\in{\mathsf{E}}^{\infty}}\varepsilon^{{\mathsf{e}}+\lambda^{+}_{j}}\mathbf{c}_{j}(u_{{\mathsf{e}}})\,K^{+}_{j}\left(\frac{x}{\varepsilon}\right)\,,

\displaystyle=u_{0}(x)+\sum_{j\geq 1}\sum_{{\mathsf{e}}\in{\mathsf{E}}^{\infty}}\varepsilon^{{\mathsf{e}}-\lambda^{-}_{j}}\mathbf{B}_{j}(U_{{\mathsf{e}}})\,K^{-}_{j}(x)\,.

We can compare these three representations by expanding each of them in the family of homogeneous harmonic functions

\{h^{+}_{k},\quad k\geq 1\}\cup\{h^{-}_{k},\quad k\geq 1\}\,.

Since the
h^{+}_{k}
and
h^{-}_{k}
are independent in
L^{2}({\mathscr{A}}_{\varepsilon}\cap\Gamma)
, we can identify the coefficients of the
h^{+}_{k}
and
h^{-}_{k}
and obtain relations between the coefficients
\mathbf{c}_{j}(u_{{\mathsf{e}}})
and
\mathbf{B}_{j}(U_{{\mathsf{e}}})
of the original series (see Lemma
6.15
below).
Owing to Theorem
4.9
and expansions (
6.14
) and (
6.15
) of
K^{\pm}_{j}
, the functions
{\mathsf{u}}_{\mathsf{e}}
,
{\mathsf{U}}_{\mathsf{e}}
,
K^{+}_{j}
, and
K^{-}_{j}
can be expanded
as follows:

\displaystyle{\mathsf{u}}_{\mathsf{e}}(x)

\displaystyle=\sum_{k\geq 1}\mathbf{c}_{k}({\mathsf{u}}_{\mathsf{e}})\,h^{+}_{k}(x)

\displaystyle{\mathsf{U}}_{\mathsf{e}}\left(\frac{x}{\varepsilon}\right)

\displaystyle=\sum_{k\geq 1}\mathbf{B}_{k}({\mathsf{U}}_{\mathsf{e}})\,h^{-}_{k}\left(\frac{x}{\varepsilon}\right)=\sum_{k\geq 1}\varepsilon^{-\lambda^{-}_{k}}\,\mathbf{B}_{k}({\mathsf{U}}_{\mathsf{e}})\,h^{-}_{k}(x)

\displaystyle K^{+}_{j}\left(\frac{x}{\varepsilon}\right)

\displaystyle=h^{+}_{j}\left(\frac{x}{\varepsilon}\right)-\sum_{k\geq 1}\mathbf{B}_{k}(Y^{+}_{j})\,h^{-}_{k}\left(\frac{x}{\varepsilon}\right)=\varepsilon^{-\lambda^{+}_{j}}\,h^{+}_{j}(x)-\sum_{k\geq 1}\varepsilon^{-\lambda^{-}_{k}}\,\mathbf{B}_{k}(Y^{+}_{j})\,h^{-}_{k}(x)

\displaystyle K^{-}_{j}(x)

\displaystyle=h^{-}_{j}(x)-\sum_{k\geq 1}\mathbf{c}_{k}(Y^{-}_{j})\,h^{+}_{k}(x).

Coming back to
u_{\varepsilon}
we obtain

\displaystyle u_{\varepsilon}(x)

\displaystyle=\sum_{{\mathsf{e}}\in{\mathsf{E}}^{\infty}}\left(\sum_{k\geq 1}\varepsilon^{{\mathsf{e}}}\mathbf{c}_{k}({\mathsf{u}}_{\mathsf{e}})\,h^{+}_{k}(x)+\sum_{k\geq 1}\varepsilon^{{\mathsf{e}}-\lambda^{-}_{k}}\,\mathbf{B}_{k}({\mathsf{U}}_{\mathsf{e}})\,h^{-}_{k}(x)\right)

\displaystyle=\sum_{k\geq 1}\varepsilon^{-\lambda^{-}_{k}}\,\mathbf{B}_{k}(U_{0})\,h^{-}_{k}(x)+\sum_{j\geq 1}\sum_{{\mathsf{e}}\in{\mathsf{E}}^{\infty}}\varepsilon^{{\mathsf{e}}+\lambda^{+}_{j}}\mathbf{c}_{j}(u_{{\mathsf{e}}})\,\left(\varepsilon^{-\lambda^{+}_{j}}\,h^{+}_{j}(x)-\sum_{k\geq 1}\varepsilon^{-\lambda^{-}_{k}}\,\mathbf{B}_{k}(Y^{+}_{j})\,h^{-}_{k}(x)\right)

\displaystyle=\sum_{k\geq 1}\mathbf{c}_{k}(u_{0})\,h^{+}_{k}(x)+\sum_{j\geq 1}\sum_{{\mathsf{e}}\in{\mathsf{E}}^{\infty}}\varepsilon^{{\mathsf{e}}-\lambda^{-}_{j}}\mathbf{B}_{j}(U_{{\mathsf{e}}})\,\left(h^{-}_{j}(x)-\sum_{k\geq 1}\mathbf{c}_{k}(Y^{-}_{j})\,h^{+}_{k}(x)\right)\,.

Recalling that all these sums are normally convergent, we can reorganize them as follows:

\displaystyle u_{\varepsilon}(x)

\displaystyle=\sum_{k\geq 1}\left(\sum_{{\mathsf{e}}\in{\mathsf{E}}^{\infty}}\varepsilon^{{\mathsf{e}}}\mathbf{c}_{k}({\mathsf{u}}_{\mathsf{e}})\right)h^{+}_{k}(x)+\sum_{k\geq 1}\left(\sum_{{\mathsf{e}}\in{\mathsf{E}}^{\infty}}\varepsilon^{{\mathsf{e}}-\lambda^{-}_{k}}\,\mathbf{B}_{k}({\mathsf{U}}_{\mathsf{e}})\right)h^{-}_{k}(x)

\displaystyle=\sum_{j\geq 1}\left(\sum_{{\mathsf{e}}\in{\mathsf{E}}^{\infty}}\varepsilon^{{\mathsf{e}}}\mathbf{c}_{j}(u_{{\mathsf{e}}})\right)h^{+}_{j}(x)+\sum_{k\geq 1}\left(\varepsilon^{-\lambda^{-}_{k}}\,\mathbf{B}_{k}(U_{0})-\sum_{j\geq 1}\sum_{{\mathsf{e}}\in{\mathsf{E}}^{\infty}}\varepsilon^{{\mathsf{e}}+\lambda^{+}_{j}-\lambda^{-}_{k}}\mathbf{c}_{j}(u_{{\mathsf{e}}})\,\mathbf{B}_{k}(Y^{+}_{j})\right)\,h^{-}_{k}(x)

\displaystyle=\sum_{k\geq 1}\left(\mathbf{c}_{k}(u_{0})-\sum_{j\geq 1}\sum_{{\mathsf{e}}\in{\mathsf{E}}^{\infty}}\varepsilon^{{\mathsf{e}}-\lambda^{-}_{j}}\mathbf{B}_{j}(U_{{\mathsf{e}}})\,\mathbf{c}_{k}(Y^{-}_{j})\right)h^{+}_{k}(x)+\sum_{j\geq 1}\left(\sum_{{\mathsf{e}}\in{\mathsf{E}}^{\infty}}\varepsilon^{{\mathsf{e}}-\lambda^{-}_{j}}\mathbf{B}_{j}(U_{{\mathsf{e}}})\right)h^{-}_{j}(x)\,.

Since the above identities are valid for all
x\in\rho\hat{\Gamma}
and all
\rho\in(2R_{0}\varepsilon_{1},r_{0}/2)
(and thus for all
x\in{\mathscr{A}}_{\varepsilon_{1}}\cap\Gamma
), and since the functions
h^{\pm}_{j}
are independent in
L^{2}({\mathscr{A}}_{\varepsilon_{1}}\cap\Gamma)
, we can identify the coefficients of the
h^{\pm}_{j}
and we find the following two independent relations:

\sum_{{\mathsf{e}}\in{\mathsf{E}}^{\infty}}\varepsilon^{{\mathsf{e}}}\mathbf{c}_{k}({\mathsf{u}}_{\mathsf{e}})=\mathbf{c}_{k}(u_{0})-\sum_{j\geq 1}\sum_{{\mathsf{e}}\in{\mathsf{E}}^{\infty}}\varepsilon^{{\mathsf{e}}-\lambda^{-}_{j}}\mathbf{B}_{j}(U_{{\mathsf{e}}})\,\mathbf{c}_{k}(Y^{-}_{j}),\qquad\forall k\geq 1

and

\sum_{{\mathsf{e}}\in{\mathsf{E}}^{\infty}}\varepsilon^{{\mathsf{e}}-\lambda^{-}_{k}}\,\mathbf{B}_{k}({\mathsf{U}}_{\mathsf{e}})=\varepsilon^{-\lambda^{-}_{k}}\,\mathbf{B}_{k}(U_{0})-\sum_{j\geq 1}\sum_{{\mathsf{e}}\in{\mathsf{E}}^{\infty}}\varepsilon^{{\mathsf{e}}+\lambda^{+}_{j}-\lambda^{-}_{k}}\mathbf{c}_{j}(u_{{\mathsf{e}}})\,\mathbf{B}_{k}(Y^{+}_{j}),\qquad\forall k\geq 1\,.

Multiplying the second equality by
\varepsilon^{\lambda^{-}_{k}}
, we obtain

\sum_{{\mathsf{e}}\in{\mathsf{E}}^{\infty}}\varepsilon^{{\mathsf{e}}}\,\mathbf{B}_{k}({\mathsf{U}}_{\mathsf{e}})=\mathbf{B}_{k}(U_{0})-\sum_{j\geq 1}\sum_{{\mathsf{e}}\in{\mathsf{E}}^{\infty}}\varepsilon^{{\mathsf{e}}+\lambda^{+}_{j}}\mathbf{c}_{j}(u_{{\mathsf{e}}})\,\mathbf{B}_{k}(Y^{+}_{j}),\qquad\forall k\geq 1\,.

Then, subtracting
\mathbf{c}_{k}(u_{0})
from the first equation and
\mathbf{B}_{k}(U_{0})
from the second, we arrive at

\sum_{{\mathsf{e}}\in{\mathsf{E}}^{\infty}\setminus\{0\}}\varepsilon^{{\mathsf{e}}}\mathbf{c}_{k}({\mathsf{u}}_{\mathsf{e}})=-\sum_{j\geq 1}\sum_{{\mathsf{e}}^{\prime}\in{\mathsf{E}}^{\infty}}\varepsilon^{{\mathsf{e}}^{\prime}-\lambda^{-}_{j}}\mathbf{B}_{j}(U_{{\mathsf{e}}^{\prime}})\,\mathbf{c}_{k}(Y^{-}_{j}),\qquad\forall k\geq 1

and

\sum_{{\mathsf{e}}\in{\mathsf{E}}^{\infty}\setminus\{0\}}\varepsilon^{{\mathsf{e}}}\,\mathbf{B}_{k}({\mathsf{U}}_{\mathsf{e}})=-\sum_{j\geq 1}\sum_{{\mathsf{e}}^{\prime}\in{\mathsf{E}}^{\infty}}\varepsilon^{{\mathsf{e}}^{\prime}+\lambda^{+}_{j}}\mathbf{c}_{j}(u_{{\mathsf{e}}^{\prime}})\,\mathbf{B}_{k}(Y^{+}_{j}),\qquad\forall k\geq 1\,.

We now identify the powers of
\varepsilon
and prove the following:

Lemma 6.15
.

With the assumptions and notations of Theorem
6.1
, and with
\mathbf{c}_{j}
and
\mathbf{B}_{j}
as defined in Theorem
4.9
, we have

(6.37a)

\mathbf{c}_{k}({\mathsf{u}}_{\mathsf{e}})=-\!\!\!\sum_{j\geq 1\;\;{\rm s.t.}\;{\mathsf{e}}+\lambda^{-}_{j}\in{\mathsf{E}}^{\infty}}\!\!\!\mathbf{B}_{j}(U_{{\mathsf{e}}+\lambda^{-}_{j}})\,\mathbf{c}_{k}(Y^{-}_{j}),\qquad\forall{\mathsf{e}}\in{\mathsf{E}}^{\infty}\setminus\{0\},\quad\forall k\geq 1

and

(6.37b)

\mathbf{B}_{k}({\mathsf{U}}_{\mathsf{e}})=-\!\!\!\sum_{j\geq 1\;\;{\rm s.t.}\;{\mathsf{e}}-\lambda^{+}_{j}\in{\mathsf{E}}^{\infty}}\!\!\!\mathbf{c}_{j}(u_{{\mathsf{e}}-\lambda^{+}_{j}})\,\mathbf{B}_{k}(Y^{+}_{j}),\qquad\forall{\mathsf{e}}\in{\mathsf{E}}^{\infty}\setminus\{0\},\quad\forall k\geq 1.

The terms present in identities (
6.37a
) and (
6.37b
) play distinctive roles: whereas the coefficients
\mathbf{c}_{k}(Y^{-}_{j})
and
\mathbf{B}_{k}(Y^{+}_{j})
only depend on
{\mathsf{P}}
and
\Omega
, and can, in this sense, be considered characteristic of the problem (see the comment after (
6.15
)), the other coefficients
\mathbf{c}_{k}({\mathsf{u}}_{\mathsf{e}})
and
\mathbf{B}_{k}({\mathsf{U}}_{\mathsf{e}})
can be considered as unknowns in a recursive system. This is clarified by writing identities (
6.37a
) and (
6.37b
) in matrix form for each
{\mathsf{e}}\in{\mathsf{E}}^{\infty}\setminus\{0\}
. To do so, we need some additional notation.

Notation 6.16

(Interaction matrices)
.

We introduce the semi-infinite square matrices
{\mathcal{S}}_{\Omega}
and
{\mathcal{S}}_{\mathsf{P}}
as follows:

•

{\mathcal{S}}_{\Omega}
is the matrix with entries
({\mathcal{S}}_{\Omega})_{kj}:=\mathbf{c}_{k}(Y^{-}_{j})
, for
k\geq 1
and
j\geq 1
;

•

{\mathcal{S}}_{\mathsf{P}}
is the matrix with entries
({\mathcal{S}}_{\mathsf{P}})_{kj}:=\mathbf{B}_{k}(Y^{+}_{j})
, for
k\geq 1
and
j\geq 1
.

Once more, we recall that the entries of the matrices
{\mathcal{S}}_{\Omega}
and
{\mathcal{S}}_{\mathsf{P}}
can be obtained from solutions of Dirichlet problems in
\Omega
and
{\mathsf{P}}
, respectively, and that they are intrinsic.

The concept of these matrices evokes the idea of scattering, if we read the expansion (
6.14
) as describing the “total” field
K^{+}_{j}
as the sum of the non-variational “incoming” field
h^{+}_{j}
and a “scattered” field that has an expansion at infinity in the basis of the “outgoing” fields
h^{-}_{j}
with expansion coefficients constituting the matrix
{\mathcal{S}}_{\mathsf{P}}
.
Similarly,
{\mathcal{S}}_{\Omega}
is related via (
6.15
) to a scattering problem at the vertex of the cone, with the roles of
h^{+}_{j}
and
h^{-}_{j}
inverted.

Notation 6.17
.

Let
C
be the vector space of real semi-infinite column vectors:
v:=\big(v_{j}\big)_{j\geq 1}
. Let
C[[\Xi^{{\mathsf{E}}^{\infty}}]]
be the (vector) space of formal series with exponent set
{\mathsf{E}}^{\infty}
and values in
C
, so that any element of
C[[\Xi^{{\mathsf{E}}^{\infty}}]]
is a formal series
{\mathfrak{a}}:=\sum_{{\mathsf{e}}\in{\mathsf{E}}^{\infty}}{\mathfrak{a}}_{\mathsf{e}}\,\Xi^{\mathsf{e}}
with
{\mathfrak{a}}_{\mathsf{e}}=\big({\mathfrak{a}}_{{\mathsf{e}};j}\big)_{j\geq 1}
(cf. Appendix
B
). We introduce the operators
\Pi^{+}
and
\Pi^{-}
on
C[[\Xi^{{\mathsf{E}}^{\infty}}]]
that take a formal series
{\mathfrak{a}}:=\sum_{{\mathsf{e}}\in{\mathsf{E}}^{\infty}}{\mathfrak{a}}_{\mathsf{e}}\,\Xi^{\mathsf{e}}
to the series
{\mathfrak{a}}^{+}=\Pi^{+}{\mathfrak{a}}
and
{\mathfrak{a}}^{-}=\Pi^{-}{\mathfrak{a}}
, respectively. These operators are defined as follows:

•

{\mathfrak{a}}^{+}
is the formal series
\sum_{{\mathsf{e}}\in{\mathsf{E}}^{\infty}}{\mathfrak{a}}^{+}_{\mathsf{e}}\,\Xi^{\mathsf{e}}
with coefficients
{\mathfrak{a}}^{+}_{\mathsf{e}}=\big({\mathfrak{a}}^{+}_{{\mathsf{e}};j}\big)_{j\geq 1}
given by

{\mathfrak{a}}^{+}_{{\mathsf{e}};j}=\begin{cases}{\mathfrak{a}}_{{\mathsf{e}}-\lambda^{+}_{j};\,j}&\mbox{if}\quad{\mathsf{e}}-\lambda^{+}_{j}\in{\mathsf{E}}^{\infty}\,,\\
0&\mbox{else.}\end{cases}

•

{\mathfrak{a}}^{-}
is the formal series
\sum_{{\mathsf{e}}\in{\mathsf{E}}^{\infty}}{\mathfrak{a}}^{-}_{\mathsf{e}}\,\Xi^{\mathsf{e}}
with coefficients
{\mathfrak{a}}^{-}_{\mathsf{e}}=\big({\mathfrak{a}}^{-}_{{\mathsf{e}};j}\big)_{j\geq 1}
given by

{\mathfrak{a}}^{-}_{{\mathsf{e}};j}=\begin{cases}{\mathfrak{a}}_{{\mathsf{e}}+\lambda^{-}_{j};\,j}&\mbox{if}\quad{\mathsf{e}}+\lambda^{-}_{j}\in{\mathsf{E}}^{\infty}\,,\\
0&\mbox{else.}\end{cases}

Then, the identities (
6.37a
) and (
6.37b
) can be written in matrix-vector products as

(6.38)

\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{c}}_{{\mathsf{e}}}={\mathcal{S}}_{\mathsf{P}}\,\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{B}}^{-}_{{\mathsf{e}}}\quad\mbox{and}\quad\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{B}}_{{\mathsf{e}}}={\mathcal{S}}_{\Omega}\,\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{c}}^{+}_{{\mathsf{e}}},\quad\forall{\mathsf{e}}\in{\mathsf{E}}^{\infty}\setminus\{0\}\,,

where
\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{c}}
and
\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{B}}
are the formal series with coefficients
\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{c}}_{\mathsf{e}}=\big(\mathbf{c}_{j}({\mathsf{u}}_{\mathsf{e}})\big)_{j\geq 1}
and
\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{B}}_{\mathsf{e}}=\big(\mathbf{B}_{j}({\mathsf{U}}_{\mathsf{e}})\big)_{j\geq 1}
, respectively.

The system of equations (
6.38
) displays the following algorithmic feature: For any
j\geq 1
, we have
{\mathsf{e}}-\lambda^{+}_{j}<{\mathsf{e}}
and
{\mathsf{e}}+\lambda^{-}_{j}<{\mathsf{e}}
. Therefore, the values of
\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{B}}^{-}_{{\mathsf{e}}}
and
\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{c}}^{+}_{{\mathsf{e}}}
only depend on coefficient of
\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{c}}
and
\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{B}}
with exponents
{\mathsf{e}}^{\prime}
that are strictly smaller than
{\mathsf{e}}
, making (
6.38
) a system that can be solved recursively. This leads to the following statement:

Theorem 6.18
.

Let
f\in\mathfrak{F}_{\Omega}
and
F\in\mathfrak{F}_{\mathsf{P}}
. Under the assumptions of Theorem
6.1
, the solution
u_{\varepsilon}
of problem (
3.4
) can be obtained for all
\varepsilon\in(0,\varepsilon_{\star})
using the following algorithm:

Define the formal series
\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{c}}
with coefficients
\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{c}}_{{\mathsf{e}}}
and
\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{B}}
with coefficients
\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{B}}_{{\mathsf{e}}}
recursively (with increasing
{\mathsf{e}}\in{\mathsf{E}}^{\infty}
) as follows:

(1)

For
{\mathsf{e}}=0
, set
\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{c}}_{0}=\big(\mathbf{c}_{j}(u_{0})\big)_{j\geq 1}
and
\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{B}}_{0}=\big(\mathbf{B}_{j}(U_{0})\big)_{j\geq 1}
, with
u_{0}
and
U_{0}
solutions of the limit problems (
6.3
).

(2)

For
{\mathsf{e}}>0
, determine
\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{c}}_{{\mathsf{e}}}
and
\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{B}}_{{\mathsf{e}}}
using (
6.38
), where the values of
\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{B}}^{-}_{{\mathsf{e}}}
and
\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{c}}^{+}_{{\mathsf{e}}}
only depend on exponents
{\mathsf{e}}^{\prime}<{\mathsf{e}}
.

Then the inner and outer expansions of
u_{\varepsilon}
are given by

U_{0}\left(\frac{x}{\varepsilon}\right)+\sum_{j\geq 1}\sum_{{\mathsf{e}}\in{\mathsf{E}}^{\infty}}\varepsilon^{{\mathsf{e}}+\lambda^{+}_{j}}\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{c}}_{{\mathsf{e}};j}\,K^{+}_{j}\left(\frac{x}{\varepsilon}\right)\,,

and

u_{0}(x)+\sum_{j\geq 1}\sum_{{\mathsf{e}}\in{\mathsf{E}}^{\infty}}\varepsilon^{{\mathsf{e}}-\lambda^{-}_{j}}\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{B}}_{{\mathsf{e}};j}\,K^{-}_{j}(x)\,,

respectively, leading to the full knowledge of
u_{\varepsilon}
.

We also note that, since the sequences
\{-\lambda^{+}_{j}\}_{j}
and
\{\lambda^{-}_{j}\}_{j}
tend to
-\infty
, for a fixed
{\mathsf{e}}\in{\mathsf{E}}^{\infty}
the vectors
\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{B}}^{-}_{{\mathsf{e}}}
and
\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{c}}^{+}_{{\mathsf{e}}}
have only a finite number of nonzero entries. Consequently, the entries
\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{c}}_{{\mathsf{e}};j}
and
\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{B}}_{{\mathsf{e}};j}
of
\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{c}}_{{\mathsf{e}}}
and
\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{B}}_{{\mathsf{e}}}
can be determined with a finite number of numerical operations.

Appendix A
Kelvin transformation

The parallel statements for the bounded and unbounded truncated cones
\Gamma\cap{\mathscr{B}}(\rho)
and
\Gamma\cap{\mathscr{B}}^{\complement}(\rho)
that we have seen in Section
4
were proved using similar reasonings and computations for both cases. There exists a clearcut method to obtain the second case directly as a corollary from the first one, by means of the
Kelvin transform
. This transform is defined using the reflection at the unit sphere
{\mathbb{S}}^{n-1}
:

x\mapsto X=|x|^{-2}x.

The Kelvin transformation
\mathcal{K}
maps a function
u
to the function
U=\mathcal{K}[u]
, defined by

(A.1)

U(X)=\mathcal{K}[u](X):=|X|^{2-n}u(|X|^{-2}X)=|x|^{n-2}u(x).

For
\rho>0
, we can use the dilation operator
\mathcal{H}_{\rho}
introduced in Section
3.1
:

\mathcal{H}_{\rho}u(x)=u(\tfrac{x}{\rho})\,,

to define the corresponding transformation
\mathcal{K}_{\rho}
associated with the reflection at the sphere of radius
\rho
:

\mathcal{K}_{\rho}:=\mathcal{H}_{\rho}\circ\mathcal{K}\circ\mathcal{H}_{\frac{1}{\rho}}\,.

This gives the formula

\mathcal{K}_{\rho}[u](X)=(\tfrac{\rho}{|X|})^{n-2}u((\tfrac{\rho}{|X|})^{2}X)\,.

Here is the basic fact:

Lemma A.1
.

Let
\rho>0
.
The Kelvin transformation
\mathcal{K}_{\rho}
associated with the reflection at the sphere of radius
\rho
is an isomorphism between

H^{1}_{0}(\Gamma\cap\overline{{\mathscr{B}}(\rho)})
and

H^{1}_{{\mathsf{w}},0}(\Gamma\cap\overline{{\mathscr{B}}^{\complement}(\rho)})
.

Proof.

Because
\mathcal{H}_{\rho}
provides isomorphisms between

H^{1}_{0}(\Gamma\cap\overline{{\mathscr{B}}(1)})
and
H^{1}_{0}(\Gamma\cap\overline{{\mathscr{B}}(\rho)})

as well as between

H^{1}_{{\mathsf{w}},0}(\Gamma\cap\overline{{\mathscr{B}}^{\complement}(1)})
and

H^{1}_{{\mathsf{w}},0}(\Gamma\cap\overline{{\mathscr{B}}^{\complement}(\rho)})
,
it is sufficient to give the proof for
\rho=1
.

Writing
X=R\vartheta
and
x=r\vartheta
, with
\vartheta\in{\mathbb{S}}^{n-1}
, we easily find that

\displaystyle|U(X)|^{2}R^{n-3}{\mathrm{d}}R

\displaystyle=|u(x)|^{2}r^{n-3}{\mathrm{d}}r\,,

\displaystyle\partial_{R}U(X)

\displaystyle=r^{n}\Big(\tfrac{2-n}{r}u(x)-\partial_{r}u(x)\Big)\,,

\displaystyle\nabla_{T}U(X)

\displaystyle=r^{n}\nabla_{T}u(x)

\displaystyle|\nabla U(X)|^{2}R^{n-1}{\mathrm{d}}R

\displaystyle=|\nabla u(x)|^{2}r^{n-1}{\mathrm{d}}r+(n-2)\partial_{r}\big(r^{n-2}u(x)^{2}\big){\mathrm{d}}r\,.

Integrating these identities for
u\in C_{0}^{\infty}(\Gamma)
, we obtain

(A.2)

\displaystyle\mathchoice{\|\hskip 0.6458pt\frac{U}{R}\hskip 0.6458pt\|_{\raise-1.80835pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle L^{2}(\Gamma\cap{\mathscr{B}}^{\complement}(1))$}}\,}{\|\frac{U}{R}\|_{L^{2}(\Gamma\cap{\mathscr{B}}^{\complement}(1))}}{\|\frac{U}{R}\|_{L^{2}(\Gamma\cap{\mathscr{B}}^{\complement}(1))}}{\|\frac{U}{R}\|_{L^{2}(\Gamma\cap{\mathscr{B}}^{\complement}(1))}}

\displaystyle=\mathchoice{\|\hskip 0.6458pt\frac{u}{r}\hskip 0.6458pt\|_{\raise-1.80835pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle L^{2}(\Gamma\cap{\mathscr{B}}(1))$}}\,}{\|\frac{u}{r}\|_{L^{2}(\Gamma\cap{\mathscr{B}}(1))}}{\|\frac{u}{r}\|_{L^{2}(\Gamma\cap{\mathscr{B}}(1))}}{\|\frac{u}{r}\|_{L^{2}(\Gamma\cap{\mathscr{B}}(1))}}\,,

(A.3)

\displaystyle\mathchoice{\|\hskip 0.6458pt\nabla U\hskip 0.6458pt\|_{\raise-1.80835pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle L^{2}(\Gamma\cap{\mathscr{B}}^{\complement}(1))$}}^{2}}{\|\nabla U\|_{L^{2}(\Gamma\cap{\mathscr{B}}^{\complement}(1))}^{2}}{\|\nabla U\|_{L^{2}(\Gamma\cap{\mathscr{B}}^{\complement}(1))}^{2}}{\|\nabla U\|_{L^{2}(\Gamma\cap{\mathscr{B}}^{\complement}(1))}^{2}}

\displaystyle=\mathchoice{\|\hskip 0.6458pt\nabla u\hskip 0.6458pt\|_{\raise-1.80835pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle L^{2}(\Gamma\cap{\mathscr{B}}(1))$}}^{2}}{\|\nabla u\|_{L^{2}(\Gamma\cap{\mathscr{B}}(1))}^{2}}{\|\nabla u\|_{L^{2}(\Gamma\cap{\mathscr{B}}(1))}^{2}}{\|\nabla u\|_{L^{2}(\Gamma\cap{\mathscr{B}}(1))}^{2}}+(n-2)\mathchoice{\|\hskip 0.6458pt\operatorname{\rm tr}_{1}u\hskip 0.6458pt\|_{\raise-1.80835pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle L^{2}(\hat{\Gamma})$}}^{2}}{\|\operatorname{\rm tr}_{1}u\|_{L^{2}(\hat{\Gamma})}^{2}}{\|\operatorname{\rm tr}_{1}u\|_{L^{2}(\hat{\Gamma})}^{2}}{\|\operatorname{\rm tr}_{1}u\|_{L^{2}(\hat{\Gamma})}^{2}}\,,

(A.4)

\displaystyle\mathchoice{\|\hskip 0.6458pt\nabla U\hskip 0.6458pt\|_{\raise-1.80835pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle L^{2}(\Gamma)$}}^{2}}{\|\nabla U\|_{L^{2}(\Gamma)}^{2}}{\|\nabla U\|_{L^{2}(\Gamma)}^{2}}{\|\nabla U\|_{L^{2}(\Gamma)}^{2}}

\displaystyle=\mathchoice{\|\hskip 0.6458pt\nabla u\hskip 0.6458pt\|_{\raise-1.80835pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle L^{2}(\Gamma)$}}^{2}}{\|\nabla u\|_{L^{2}(\Gamma)}^{2}}{\|\nabla u\|_{L^{2}(\Gamma)}^{2}}{\|\nabla u\|_{L^{2}(\Gamma)}^{2}}\,.

Then, combining (
A.3
) with the estimate (
4.5
) from Lemma
4.2
, we obtain that

\mathchoice{\|\hskip 0.6458pt\nabla u\hskip 0.6458pt\|_{\raise-1.80835pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle L^{2}(\Gamma\cap{\mathscr{B}}(1))$}}^{2}}{\|\nabla u\|_{L^{2}(\Gamma\cap{\mathscr{B}}(1))}^{2}}{\|\nabla u\|_{L^{2}(\Gamma\cap{\mathscr{B}}(1))}^{2}}{\|\nabla u\|_{L^{2}(\Gamma\cap{\mathscr{B}}(1))}^{2}}\leq\mathchoice{\|\hskip 0.6458pt\nabla U\hskip 0.6458pt\|_{\raise-1.80835pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle L^{2}(\Gamma\cap{\mathscr{B}}^{\complement}(1))$}}^{2}}{\|\nabla U\|_{L^{2}(\Gamma\cap{\mathscr{B}}^{\complement}(1))}^{2}}{\|\nabla U\|_{L^{2}(\Gamma\cap{\mathscr{B}}^{\complement}(1))}^{2}}{\|\nabla U\|_{L^{2}(\Gamma\cap{\mathscr{B}}^{\complement}(1))}^{2}}\leq(1+(n-2)C_{\hat{\Gamma}}^{2})\mathchoice{\|\hskip 0.6458pt\nabla u\hskip 0.6458pt\|_{\raise-1.80835pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle L^{2}(\Gamma\cap{\mathscr{B}}(1))$}}^{2}}{\|\nabla u\|_{L^{2}(\Gamma\cap{\mathscr{B}}(1))}^{2}}{\|\nabla u\|_{L^{2}(\Gamma\cap{\mathscr{B}}(1))}^{2}}{\|\nabla u\|_{L^{2}(\Gamma\cap{\mathscr{B}}(1))}^{2}}\,,

which implies the equivalence of the
H^{1}
seminorms of
u\in C_{0}^{\infty}(\Gamma)
and its transform
U
. Observing that
\mathcal{K}
maps
C_{0}^{\infty}(\Gamma)
to itself, and using a standard density argument, we obtain the equivalence of the norms in
H^{1}_{0}(\Gamma\cap\overline{{\mathscr{B}}(1)})
of
u
and in
H^{1}_{{\mathsf{w}},0}(\Gamma\cap\overline{{\mathscr{B}}^{\complement}(1)})
of
\mathcal{K}[u]
, thus proving that

\mathcal{K}
is an isomorphism between

H^{1}_{0}(\Gamma\cap\overline{{\mathscr{B}}(1)})
and
H^{1}_{{\mathsf{w}},0}(\Gamma\cap\overline{{\mathscr{B}}^{\complement}(1)})
, with inverse given by the same formula.
∎

For example, Lemma
A.1
can be used to deduce the trace estimate (
4.6
) directly from (
4.5
). It suffices to notice that the traces on
\rho\hat{\Gamma}
of
u
and its transform
\mathcal{K}_{\rho}[u]
are the same.

Another straightforward consequence of equation (
A.4
) is the well-known classical relation:

\Delta\mathcal{K}[u]=\mathcal{K}[|x|^{4}\Delta u]\,,

which shows that
\mathcal{K}
preserves harmonic functions.

For the special harmonic functions
h^{\pm}_{j}
, which form the bases for the expansion of harmonic functions near the origin or near infinity, we have the relation

(A.5)

h^{-}_{j}=\mathcal{K}[h^{+}_{j}]\,.

Appendix B
Generalized power series

B.1.
Formal generalized power series

In this paper, we come across series of the form

\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}c_{j}\xi^{{\mathsf{e}}_{j}}

where
\{{\mathsf{e}}_{j}\}_{j=0}^{\infty}
is a sequence of nonnegative real exponents and
\{c_{j}\}_{j=0}^{\infty}
is a sequence of coefficients in a suitable space of functions or operators.

The study of such series has a rich history in algebra, with early contributions by Hahn
[
18
]
, MacLane
[
30
]
, Mal’tsev
[
31
]
, and Neumann
[
36
]
. Over time, the theory surrounding these series has evolved and branched out into various directions, connecting with concepts such as formal Laurent series, surreal numbers, and the Levi-Civita field, among other algebraic structures. In particular, the generalization has concerned the set of exponents, that can be an unspecified ordered monoid (i.e. ordered additive semigroup with a zero element).

For this paper, we do not require the full generality provided in the algebraic theory. Only a few elementary facts will be sufficient. In particular, we will confine ourselves to generalized power series with exponents in
[0,+\infty)
, which for this purpose we will denote by
{\mathsf{E}}_{+}
.
Although the results in this section are well known, for the sake of convenience of the reader, we give some relevant proofs.

A
generalized power series

{\mathfrak{f}}:=\sum_{{\mathsf{e}}\in{\mathsf{E}}}c_{\mathsf{e}}\Xi^{{\mathsf{e}}}

with exponents in a set
{\mathsf{E}}
, coefficients in
C
and indeterminate
\Xi
is simply a mapping
{\mathsf{e}}\mapsto c_{{\mathsf{e}}}
from
{\mathsf{E}}
to
C
. When the set
C
has additional structure, such as that of a group or ring, the set of such series will acquire an analogous structure, as suggested by the writing as sums.
In general, we assume that
C
is an additive group with neutral element
0
.
The subset

{\mathsf{E}}_{\mathfrak{f}}:=\{{\mathsf{e}}\in{\mathsf{E}},\quad c_{\mathsf{e}}\neq\text{0}\}

is called the support of
{\mathfrak{f}}
. Let us first recall:

Definition B.1
.

A totally ordered set
{\mathsf{E}}
is said
well-ordered
if every nonempty subset of
{\mathsf{E}}
has a smallest element.

Remark B.2
.

The proofs of the following statements are easy exercises.

(i) Any well-ordered subset of
{\mathsf{E}}_{+}
is countable.

(ii) Any discrete subset of
{\mathsf{E}}_{+}
(i.e. a set without finite accumulation points) is well-ordered.

(iii) There exist well-ordered sets that are not discrete (for example containing an increasing converging sequence).

(iv) A non-empty totally ordered set
{\mathsf{E}}
is well-ordered if and only if it does not contain an infinite strictly decreasing sequence or equivalently, every sequence in
{\mathsf{E}}
contains an increasing subsequence.

Definition B.3
.

Let
C
be an additive group. The set of generalized power series
{\mathfrak{f}}
with exponents in
{\mathsf{E}}\subset{\mathsf{E}}_{+}
and coefficients in
C
such that

(B.1)

\mbox{ the support }{\mathsf{E}}_{{\mathfrak{f}}}\mbox{ is well-ordered}

is denoted by
C[[\Xi^{{\mathsf{E}}}]]
.

If
{\mathsf{E}}\subset{\mathsf{E}}^{\prime}
, then
C[[\Xi^{{\mathsf{E}}}]]
is in a natural way contained in
C[[\Xi^{{\mathsf{E}}^{\prime}}]]
(extension by zero), so that all the generalized power series we will consider here belong to
C[[\Xi^{{\mathsf{E}}_{+}}]]
.

The group structure of
C
gives
C[[\Xi^{{\mathsf{E}}_{+}}]]
a group structure:
If
{\mathfrak{f}}:=\sum_{{\mathsf{e}}\in{\mathsf{E}}}c_{\mathsf{e}}\Xi^{{\mathsf{e}}}
and

{\mathfrak{f}}^{\prime}:=\sum_{{\mathsf{e}}\in{\mathsf{E}}^{\prime}}c_{\mathsf{e}}^{\prime}\Xi^{{\mathsf{e}}}
, then

{\mathfrak{f}}+{\mathfrak{f}}^{\prime}:=\sum_{{\mathsf{e}}\in{\mathsf{E}}\cup{\mathsf{E}}^{\prime}}(c_{\mathsf{e}}+c_{{\mathsf{e}}}^{\prime})\Xi^{{\mathsf{e}}}\,.

The important observation is that
{\mathsf{E}}_{{\mathfrak{f}}+{\mathfrak{f}}^{\prime}}\subset{\mathsf{E}}_{{\mathfrak{f}}}\cup{\mathsf{E}}_{{\mathfrak{f}}^{\prime}}
, and this is well-ordered if
{\mathsf{E}}_{{\mathfrak{f}}}
and
{\mathsf{E}}_{{\mathfrak{f}}^{\prime}}
are well-ordered. The neutral element of
C[[\Xi^{{\mathsf{E}}_{+}}]]
for the addition is the null series with empty support.

Similarly, the structure of a vector space over a field
K
carries over from
C
to
C[[\Xi^{{\mathsf{E}}_{+}}]]
.

If
C
is a ring, then
C[[\Xi^{{\mathsf{E}}_{+}}]]
has a ring structure, too: The product is defined by

{\mathfrak{f}}\,{\mathfrak{f}}^{\prime}=\sum_{{\mathsf{e}}\in{\mathsf{E}}+{\mathsf{E}}^{\prime}}\Big(\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}{\mathsf{e}}_{1}+{\mathsf{e}}_{2}={\mathsf{e}}\\
{\mathsf{e}}_{1}\in{\mathsf{E}},{\mathsf{e}}_{2}\in{\mathsf{E}}^{\prime}\end{subarray}}c_{{\mathsf{e}}_{1}}c_{{\mathsf{e}}_{2}}^{\prime}\Big)\Xi^{\mathsf{e}}\,.

For the supports one has

{\mathsf{E}}_{{\mathfrak{f}}\,{\mathfrak{f}}^{\prime}}\subset{\mathsf{E}}_{{\mathfrak{f}}}+{\mathsf{E}}_{{\mathfrak{f}}^{\prime}}=\{{\mathsf{e}}_{1}+{\mathsf{e}}_{2},\quad{\mathsf{e}}_{1}\in{\mathsf{E}}_{{\mathfrak{f}}},\;{\mathsf{e}}_{2}\in{\mathsf{E}}_{{\mathfrak{f}}^{\prime}}\}\,.

At this point, the condition of the well-ordering of the supports becomes essential.

Lemma B.4
.

Let
{\mathsf{E}}_{1},{\mathsf{E}}_{2}
be well-ordered subsets of
{\mathsf{E}}_{+}=[0,\infty)
. Then

{\mathsf{E}}_{1}+{\mathsf{E}}_{2}
is well-ordered, and for any
{\mathsf{e}}\in{\mathsf{E}}_{1}+{\mathsf{E}}_{2}
the set

\{({\mathsf{e}}_{1},{\mathsf{e}}_{2})\in{\mathsf{E}}_{1}\times{\mathsf{E}}_{2},\quad{\mathsf{e}}_{1}\in{\mathsf{E}}_{1},\;{\mathsf{e}}_{2}\in{\mathsf{E}}_{2},\;{\mathsf{e}}_{1}+{\mathsf{e}}_{2}={\mathsf{e}}\}

is finite.

Proof.

We use Remark
B.2
, (iv).
Suppose that
({\mathsf{e}}_{j})_{j\in{\mathbb{N}}}
is a sequence in
{\mathsf{E}}_{1}+{\mathsf{E}}_{2}
with

{\mathsf{e}}_{j}={\mathsf{e}}_{j1}+{\mathsf{e}}_{j2}
,
{\mathsf{e}}_{jk}\in{\mathsf{E}}_{k}
. The sequence
({\mathsf{e}}_{j1})_{j}
contains an increasing subsequence
({\mathsf{e}}_{j_{\ell}1})_{\ell}
. If
({\mathsf{e}}_{j})_{j\in{\mathbb{N}}}
were strictly decreasing, then
({\mathsf{e}}_{j_{\ell}2})_{\ell}
would also be strictly decreasing, in contradiction with the well-orderedness of
{\mathsf{E}}_{2}
. Hence
{\mathsf{E}}_{1}+{\mathsf{E}}_{2}
is well-ordered.
Suppose now there exists
{\mathsf{e}}\in{\mathsf{E}}_{1}+{\mathsf{E}}_{2}
with an infinite number of different decompositions

{\mathsf{e}}={\mathsf{e}}_{j1}+{\mathsf{e}}_{j2}
,
j\in{\mathbb{N}}
,
{\mathsf{e}}_{jk}\in{\mathsf{E}}_{k}
. We may assume that
({\mathsf{e}}_{j1})_{j}
is strictly increasing, implying the contradiction that
({\mathsf{e}}_{j2})_{j}
is strictly decreasing.
∎

The ring
C
is naturally embedded in
C[[\Xi^{{\mathsf{E}}_{+}}]]
by identifying
c_{0}\in C
with the series
c_{0}\Xi^{0}
, written simply as
c_{0}
. If
C
has a left and right multiplicative identity
1
, then this yields a multiplicative identity for
C[[\Xi^{{\mathsf{E}}_{+}}]]
as well, which we denote with the same symbol
1
.
One can then talk about (left and right) inverses of formal generalized power series.

Before stating the main result of this section, we need a lemma that makes use of the archimedian property of
{\mathsf{E}}_{+}=[0,\infty)
.

Lemma B.5
.

Let
{\mathsf{E}}
be a well-ordered subset of
{\mathsf{E}}_{+}
and let

{\mathsf{E}}^{\infty}=\{{\mathsf{e}}={\mathsf{e}}_{1}+\ldots+{\mathsf{e}}_{k},\quad{\mathsf{e}}_{1},\ldots,{\mathsf{e}}_{k}\in{\mathsf{E}}\cup\{0\},\quad k\in{\mathbb{N}}^{*}\}.

be the monoid generated by
{\mathsf{E}}
.
Then
{\mathsf{E}}^{\infty}
is well-ordered.

Proof.

Let
{\mathsf{e}}_{*}
be the smallest nonzero element of
{\mathsf{E}}
. If no such element exists, then
{\mathsf{E}}^{\infty}=\{0\}
, which is well-ordered.
Let
S\subset{\mathsf{E}}^{\infty}
and
{\mathsf{e}}_{0}\in S
. To prove that
S
has a smallest element, it suffices to prove the same for
S^{\prime}:=\{{\mathsf{e}}\in S,\quad{\mathsf{e}}\leq{\mathsf{e}}_{0}\}
.
Since
{\mathsf{E}}_{+}
is archimedian, there exists
k\in{\mathbb{N}}
such that
k{\mathsf{e}}_{*}>{\mathsf{e}}_{0}
.
Let

{\mathsf{E}}^{k}=\{{\mathsf{e}}_{1}+\ldots+{\mathsf{e}}_{k},\quad{\mathsf{e}}_{1},\ldots,{\mathsf{e}}_{k}\in{\mathsf{E}}\cup\{0\}\,\}\,.

Then
S^{\prime}\subset{\mathsf{E}}^{k}
, and since
{\mathsf{E}}^{k}
is well-ordered according to Lemma
B.4
,
S^{\prime}
has a smallest element.
∎

Theorem B.6
.

Let
C
be a ring with identity
1
. Let
{\mathfrak{f}}\in C[[\Xi^{{\mathsf{E}}_{+}}]]
with

0\not\in{\mathsf{E}}_{{\mathfrak{f}}}
. Then
\text{1}+{\mathfrak{f}}
is invertible in
C[[\Xi^{{\mathsf{E}}_{+}}]]
with inverse given by the formal Neumann series

(B.2)

(\text{1}+{\mathfrak{f}})^{-1}:=\text{1}+\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}(-{\mathfrak{f}})^{k}=\text{1}+\sum_{{\mathsf{e}}\in{\mathsf{E}}_{{\mathfrak{f}}}^{\infty}}\Big(\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}(-1)^{k}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}{\mathsf{e}}_{1}+\dots+{\mathsf{e}}_{k}={\mathsf{e}}\\
{\mathsf{e}}_{1},\dots,{\mathsf{e}}_{k}\in{\mathsf{E}}_{{\mathfrak{f}}}\end{subarray}}c_{{\mathsf{e}}_{1}}\dots c_{{\mathsf{e}}_{k}}\Big)\Xi^{\mathsf{e}}

with support contained in the monoid
{\mathsf{E}}_{{\mathfrak{f}}}^{\infty}
.

Proof.

This is a well-defined formal generalized power series, because for any
{\mathsf{e}}\in{\mathsf{E}}_{{\mathfrak{f}}}^{\infty}
, the sum defining the coefficient of
\Xi^{{\mathsf{e}}}
is finite, see Lemma
B.4
. The proof is then the usual identity:

{\mathfrak{f}}\big(\text{1}+\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}(-{\mathfrak{f}})^{k}\big)=\big(\text{1}+\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}(-{\mathfrak{f}})^{k}\big){\mathfrak{f}}=\text{1}-\big(\text{1}+\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}(-{\mathfrak{f}})^{k}\big)\,.

∎

Corollary B.7
.

Let
{\mathfrak{f}}=\sum_{{\mathsf{e}}}c_{{\mathsf{e}}}\Xi^{{\mathsf{e}}}\in C[[\Xi^{{\mathsf{E}}_{+}}]]
. Then
{\mathfrak{f}}
is invertible in
C[[\Xi^{{\mathsf{E}}_{+}}]]
if and only if
c_{0}
is invertible in
C
.

Proof.

If
{\mathfrak{f}}
is invertible, then the constant term in
{\mathfrak{f}}^{-1}
is the inverse of
c_{0}
.
If
c_{0}
is invertible, write

c_{0}^{-1}{\mathfrak{f}}=\text{1}+\sum_{{\mathsf{e}}\neq 0}c_{0}^{-1}c_{{\mathsf{e}}}\Xi^{{\mathsf{e}}}

and apply the theorem.
∎

Remark B.8
.

The infinite series
\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}(-{\mathfrak{f}})^{k}
converges formally in the sense that for exponents below a given finite threshold, only a finite number of terms in the series are contributing. This type of formal convergence can be made into a convergence with respect to a metric if one introduces the
valuation

v({\mathfrak{f}})
of a generalized power series

{\mathfrak{f}}\in C[[\Xi^{{\mathsf{E}}_{+}}]]
and then the
modulus

|{\mathfrak{f}}|
(not a norm!) by looking at the smallest exponent with non-zero coefficient.

(B.3)

v({\mathfrak{f}}):=\min{\mathsf{E}}_{{\mathfrak{f}}}\,;\quad|{\mathfrak{f}}|:=e^{-v({\mathfrak{f}})}\mbox{ if }{\mathfrak{f}}\neq\text{0}\,,\;|\text{0}|:=0\,.

One has
|{\mathfrak{f}}+{\mathfrak{g}}|\leq\max\{|{\mathfrak{f}}|,|{\mathfrak{g}}|\}
, and with the metric
d({\mathfrak{f}},{\mathfrak{g}})=|{\mathfrak{f}}-{\mathfrak{g}}|
,
C[[\Xi^{{\mathsf{E}}_{+}}]]
becomes a
complete ultra-metric space
. In particular, if
0\not\in{\mathsf{E}}_{{\mathfrak{f}}}
as in Theorem
B.6
, then
|{\mathfrak{f}}|<1
, and because of
|{\mathfrak{f}}\,{\mathfrak{g}}|\leq|{\mathfrak{f}}||{\mathfrak{g}}|
and hence
|(-{\mathfrak{f}})^{k}|\leq|{\mathfrak{f}}|^{k}
, the convergence of the series
\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}(-{\mathfrak{f}})^{k}
follows.

This type of convergence is, however,
not
the convergence that we will study in the next section.

B.2.
Convergent generalized power series

If the coefficients
C
are nonnegative reals,
C={\mathsf{E}}_{+}
, a formal power series

{\mathfrak{f}}=\sum_{{\mathsf{e}}}c_{{\mathsf{e}}}\Xi^{{\mathsf{e}}}\in C[[\Xi^{{\mathsf{E}}_{+}}]]
can be evaluated at
\xi>0
:

{\mathfrak{f}}(\xi)=\sum_{{\mathsf{e}}\in{\mathsf{E}}_{{\mathfrak{f}}}}c_{{\mathsf{e}}}\xi^{{\mathsf{e}}}\in[0,\infty].

The value of the sum does not depend on the order of summation, and the function

\xi\mapsto{\mathfrak{f}}(\xi)
is monotone. In particular, if
{\mathfrak{f}}(\xi)<\infty
for some
\xi>0
, then
{\mathfrak{f}}(\eta)<\infty
for
0<\eta\leq\xi
. Additionally, in this case,
\eta\mapsto{\mathfrak{f}}(\eta)
is continuous on
(0,\xi]
and has a continuous extension to
[0,\xi]
, because

\lim_{\eta\to 0}{\mathfrak{f}}(\eta)=c_{0}\,.

Note that
{\mathsf{E}}_{{\mathfrak{f}}}
is a well-ordered subset of
[0,\infty)
and therefore countable, so that the series defining
{\mathfrak{f}}(\xi)
can be understood in the usual way as the limit of finite partial sums. It is also normally convergent on
[0,\xi]
in the sense that

\sum_{{\mathsf{e}}\in{\mathsf{E}}_{{\mathfrak{f}}}}\sup_{\eta\in[0,\xi]}c_{{\mathsf{e}}}\eta^{{\mathsf{e}}}<\infty\,,

because
\sup_{\eta\in[0,\xi]}c_{{\mathsf{e}}}\eta^{{\mathsf{e}}}=c_{{\mathsf{e}}}\xi^{{\mathsf{e}}}
.

These properties can be used to bound series with coefficients in a normed vector space.
Let
C
be a normed vector space with norm
\mathchoice{\|\hskip 0.6458pt\cdot\hskip 0.6458pt\|_{\raise-1.80835pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle$}}\,}{\|\cdot\|}{\|\cdot\|}{\|\cdot\|}
. The mapping
N
defined by

N\Big(\sum_{{\mathsf{e}}}c_{{\mathsf{e}}}\Xi^{{\mathsf{e}}}\Big)=\sum_{{\mathsf{e}}}\|c_{{\mathsf{e}}}\|\Xi^{{\mathsf{e}}}

associates a generalized power series
{\mathfrak{f}}=\sum_{{\mathsf{e}}}c_{{\mathsf{e}}}\Xi^{{\mathsf{e}}}
in
C[[\Xi^{{\mathsf{E}}_{+}}]]
with a generalized power series
N({\mathfrak{f}})\in{\mathsf{E}}_{+}[[\Xi^{{\mathsf{E}}_{+}}]]
. The support of
N({\mathfrak{f}})
is the same as the support of
{\mathfrak{f}}
.

Definition B.9
.

We call
{\mathfrak{f}}\in C[[\Xi^{{\mathsf{E}}_{+}}]]
a
convergent generalized power series
if there exists
\xi>0
such that

N({\mathfrak{f}})(\xi)<\infty
.
The set of convergent generalized power series with exponents in
{\mathsf{E}}
is denoted by
C[[\Xi^{{\mathsf{E}}}]]_{*}
, and for
\xi>0
, we write
C[[\Xi^{{\mathsf{E}}}]]_{\xi}
for the subset of those
{\mathfrak{f}}
for which
N({\mathfrak{f}})(\xi)<\infty
.

An example of a convergent generalized power series is a series that satisfies a polynomial growth estimate for the exponents and an exponential growth estimate for the coefficients. In what follows, we write

\#\{{\mathsf{e}}<b,\quad{\mathsf{e}}\in{\mathsf{E}}\}
for the number of exponents in
{\mathsf{E}}\subset{\mathsf{E}}_{+}
that are smaller than
b
. This number is finite for all
b>0
if and only if
{\mathsf{E}}
is discrete.

Lemma B.10
.

Let
{\mathfrak{f}}=\sum_{{\mathsf{e}}\in{\mathsf{E}}}c_{{\mathsf{e}}}\Xi^{{\mathsf{e}}}\in C[[\Xi^{{\mathsf{E}}_{+}}]]
and assume that there are positive constants
a
,
d
,
\rho
,
m
such that

(B.4)

\#\{{\mathsf{e}}<b,\quad{\mathsf{e}}\in{\mathsf{E}}\}\leq a(b+1)^{m}\>\mbox{ for all }b>0\quad\mbox{ and }\quad\mathchoice{\|\hskip 0.6458ptc_{{\mathsf{e}}}\hskip 0.6458pt\|_{\raise-1.80835pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle$}}\,}{\|c_{{\mathsf{e}}}\|}{\|c_{{\mathsf{e}}}\|}{\|c_{{\mathsf{e}}}\|}\leq d\,\rho^{{\mathsf{e}}}\>\mbox{ for all }{\mathsf{e}}\in{\mathsf{E}}\,.

Then
{\mathfrak{f}}\in C[[\Xi^{{\mathsf{E}}}]]_{\xi}\,
for all
\xi\in(0,\frac{1}{\rho})
.

Proof.

For
k\in{\mathbb{N}}
, the number of exponents in the interval
[k,k+1)
is bounded by

\#\{{\mathsf{e}}<k+1,\quad{\mathsf{e}}\in{\mathsf{E}}\}\leq a(k+2)^{m}
and the norm
\mathchoice{\|\hskip 0.6458ptc_{e}\hskip 0.6458pt\|_{\raise-1.80835pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle$}}\,}{\|c_{e}\|}{\|c_{e}\|}{\|c_{e}\|}
of the corresponding coefficients by
d\,\max\{\rho^{k},\rho^{k+1}\}
. Therefore, for any
\xi>0
we have

N({\mathfrak{f}})(\xi)=\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\sum_{k\leq{\mathsf{e}}<k+1}\mathchoice{\|\hskip 0.6458ptc_{e}\hskip 0.6458pt\|_{\raise-1.80835pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle$}}\,}{\|c_{e}\|}{\|c_{e}\|}{\|c_{e}\|}\xi^{e}\leq\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}a\,d\,\max\{1,\tfrac{1}{\rho}\}(k+2)^{m}(\rho\xi)^{k+1}\,.

This is convergent as soon as
\rho\xi<1
.
∎

If
C
is a Banach space and
{\mathfrak{f}}\in C[[\Xi^{{\mathsf{E}}}]]_{\xi}
, then
the series
{\mathfrak{f}}(\xi)=\sum_{{\mathsf{e}}\in{\mathsf{E}}_{{\mathfrak{f}}}}c_{{\mathsf{e}}}\xi^{{\mathsf{e}}}
converges in
C
, and
one has
\mathchoice{\|\hskip 0.6458pt{\mathfrak{f}}(\xi)\hskip 0.6458pt\|_{\raise-1.80835pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle$}}\,}{\|{\mathfrak{f}}(\xi)\|}{\|{\mathfrak{f}}(\xi)\|}{\|{\mathfrak{f}}(\xi)\|}\leq N({\mathfrak{f}})(\xi)
.
Then, the series

{\mathfrak{f}}(\eta)=\sum_{{\mathsf{e}}}c_{{\mathsf{e}}}\eta^{{\mathsf{e}}}

converges normally in
C
for all
\eta\in[0,\xi]
. It follows that the sum does not depend on the order of summation, that
\mathchoice{\|\hskip 0.6458pt{\mathfrak{f}}(\eta)\hskip 0.6458pt\|_{\raise-1.80835pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle$}}\,}{\|{\mathfrak{f}}(\eta)\|}{\|{\mathfrak{f}}(\eta)\|}{\|{\mathfrak{f}}(\eta)\|}\leq N({\mathfrak{f}})(\xi)
, and that
\eta\mapsto{\mathfrak{f}}(\eta)
is a continuous function on
[0,\xi]
with values in
C
. In particular,
{\mathfrak{f}}(\eta)\to c_{0}
in
C
as
\eta\to 0
.

It is not hard to see that

\mathchoice{\|\hskip 0.6458pt{\mathfrak{f}}\hskip 0.6458pt\|_{\raise-1.80835pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\xi$}}\,}{\|{\mathfrak{f}}\|_{\xi}}{\|{\mathfrak{f}}\|_{\xi}}{\|{\mathfrak{f}}\|_{\xi}}:=N({\mathfrak{f}})(\xi)

is a norm that makes
C[[\Xi^{{\mathsf{E}}}]]_{\xi}
a normed vector space. If
{\mathsf{E}}
is well-ordered, then one can readily show that
C[[\Xi^{{\mathsf{E}}}]]_{\xi}
is a Banach space, and if
C
is a Banach algebra, then
\mathchoice{\|\hskip 0.6458pt\cdot\hskip 0.6458pt\|_{\raise-1.80835pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\xi$}}\,}{\|\cdot\|_{\xi}}{\|\cdot\|_{\xi}}{\|\cdot\|_{\xi}}
makes
C[[\Xi^{{\mathsf{E}}}]]_{\xi}
a Banach algebra.
In particular, we have

(B.5)

\mathchoice{\|\hskip 0.6458pt{\mathfrak{f}}\,{\mathfrak{g}}\hskip 0.6458pt\|_{\raise-1.80835pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\xi$}}\,}{\|{\mathfrak{f}}\,{\mathfrak{g}}\|_{\xi}}{\|{\mathfrak{f}}\,{\mathfrak{g}}\|_{\xi}}{\|{\mathfrak{f}}\,{\mathfrak{g}}\|_{\xi}}\leq\mathchoice{\|\hskip 0.6458pt{\mathfrak{f}}\hskip 0.6458pt\|_{\raise-1.80835pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\xi$}}\,}{\|{\mathfrak{f}}\|_{\xi}}{\|{\mathfrak{f}}\|_{\xi}}{\|{\mathfrak{f}}\|_{\xi}}\mathchoice{\|\hskip 0.6458pt{\mathfrak{g}}\hskip 0.6458pt\|_{\raise-1.80835pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\xi$}}\,}{\|{\mathfrak{g}}\|_{\xi}}{\|{\mathfrak{g}}\|_{\xi}}{\|{\mathfrak{g}}\|_{\xi}}.

We observe that to prove the completeness of
C[[\Xi^{{\mathsf{E}}}]]_{\xi}
, we cannot relax the assumption that
{\mathsf{E}}
is well-ordered. As an example, consider
{\mathsf{E}}={\mathsf{E}}_{+}
and let
{\mathfrak{f}}_{N}:=\sum_{j=1}^{N}2^{-j}\Xi^{1/j}
. We can verify that
\{{\mathfrak{f}}_{N}\}_{N=1}^{\infty}
is a Cauchy sequence in
{\mathbb{R}}[[\Xi^{{\mathsf{E}}_{+}}]]_{\xi}
for any given
\xi>0
, but the support of the limiting series
\sum_{j=1}^{\infty}2^{-j}\Xi^{1/j}
is not well-ordered.

For our applications, we need two further results on convergent generalized power series, for which we will provide proofs.

The first is the
identity principle
, that says that the coefficients of a convergent generalized power series
{\mathfrak{f}}
can be recovered from the function
\xi\mapsto{\mathfrak{f}}(\xi)
.

Proposition B.11
.

Let
C
be a Banach space. Let
{\mathfrak{f}},{\mathfrak{g}}\in C[[\Xi^{\mathsf{E}}]]_{*}
. If there exists a sequence
\{\xi_{i}\}_{i}
of positive real numbers such that
\xi_{i}\to 0
and
{\mathfrak{f}}(\xi_{i})={\mathfrak{g}}(\xi_{i})
for all
i\geq 1
, then
{\mathfrak{f}}={\mathfrak{g}}
.

Proof.

By considering the difference
{\mathfrak{f}}-{\mathfrak{g}}
, we see that it suffices to give the proof for the case
{\mathfrak{g}}=\text{0}
. So let
{\mathfrak{f}}(\xi_{i})=\text{0}
for all
i
. If
{\mathfrak{f}}\neq 0
, its support
{\mathsf{E}}_{{\mathfrak{f}}}
is not empty and has a smallest element
{\mathsf{e}}_{1}
. Let
c_{1}
be the corresponding coefficient. Set
{\mathfrak{f}}_{1}:=\sum_{{\mathsf{e}}}c_{{\mathsf{e}}}\Xi^{{\mathsf{e}}-{\mathsf{e}}_{1}}
. Then
{\mathfrak{f}}_{1}\in C[[\Xi^{{\mathsf{E}}_{+}}]]_{*}
with
f_{1}(\xi_{i})=\xi_{i}^{-{\mathsf{e}}_{1}}{\mathfrak{f}}(\xi_{i})=0
for all
i
, and we arrive at the contradiction

c_{1}=\lim_{\xi\to 0}{\mathfrak{f}}_{1}(\xi)=\text{0}\,.

∎

The second and most important result concerns the inverse of a convergent generalized power series.

Theorem B.12
.

Let
C
be a Banach algebra and let
{\mathfrak{f}}=\sum_{{\mathsf{e}}\in{\mathsf{E}}}c_{{\mathsf{e}}}\Xi^{{\mathsf{e}}}\in C[[\Xi^{{\mathsf{E}}_{+}}]]_{*}
be
a convergent generalized power series with
{\mathsf{E}}\subset{\mathsf{E}}_{+}
. Assume that
\min{\mathsf{E}}_{{\mathfrak{f}}}>0
and

\mathchoice{\|\hskip 0.6458pt{\mathfrak{f}}\hskip 0.6458pt\|_{\raise-1.80835pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\xi$}}\,}{\|{\mathfrak{f}}\|_{\xi}}{\|{\mathfrak{f}}\|_{\xi}}{\|{\mathfrak{f}}\|_{\xi}}<1\quad\mbox{ for some }\xi>0\,.

Then the formal power series
(\text{1}+{\mathfrak{f}})^{-1}
, which exists according to Theorem
B.6
, is convergent and thus an element of
C[[\Xi^{{\mathsf{E}}_{{\mathfrak{f}}}^{\infty}}]]_{\xi}
.

Proof.

The Neumann series

(\text{1}+{\mathfrak{f}}(\xi))^{-1}=\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\big(-{\mathfrak{f}}(\xi)\big)^{k}

converges in
C
, because we have
\mathchoice{\|\hskip 0.6458pt{\mathfrak{f}}(\xi)\hskip 0.6458pt\|_{\raise-1.80835pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle$}}\,}{\|{\mathfrak{f}}(\xi)\|}{\|{\mathfrak{f}}(\xi)\|}{\|{\mathfrak{f}}(\xi)\|}\leq\mathchoice{\|\hskip 0.6458pt{\mathfrak{f}}\hskip 0.6458pt\|_{\raise-1.80835pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\xi$}}\,}{\|{\mathfrak{f}}\|_{\xi}}{\|{\mathfrak{f}}\|_{\xi}}{\|{\mathfrak{f}}\|_{\xi}}<1
and

\mathchoice{\|\hskip 0.6458pt(-{\mathfrak{f}}(\xi))^{k}\hskip 0.6458pt\|_{\raise-1.80835pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle$}}\,}{\|(-{\mathfrak{f}}(\xi))^{k}\|}{\|(-{\mathfrak{f}}(\xi))^{k}\|}{\|(-{\mathfrak{f}}(\xi))^{k}\|}\leq\|{\mathfrak{f}}\|_{\xi}^{k}
(see (
B.5
)). According to the expression for the powers
{\mathfrak{f}}^{k}
in (
B.2
), we have

\big({\mathfrak{f}}(\xi)\big)^{k}=({\mathfrak{f}}^{k})(\xi)\,.

It follows that

(1+{\mathfrak{f}}(\xi))^{-1}=\big((\text{1}+{\mathfrak{f}})^{-1}\big)(\xi)

and

\mathchoice{\|\hskip 0.6458pt\big((\text{1}+{\mathfrak{f}})^{-1}\big)\hskip 0.6458pt\|_{\raise-1.80835pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\xi$}}\,}{\|\big((\text{1}+{\mathfrak{f}})^{-1}\big)\|_{\xi}}{\|\big((\text{1}+{\mathfrak{f}})^{-1}\big)\|_{\xi}}{\|\big((\text{1}+{\mathfrak{f}})^{-1}\big)\|_{\xi}}\leq\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\|{\mathfrak{f}}\|_{\xi}^{k}=\big(1-\mathchoice{\|\hskip 0.6458pt{\mathfrak{f}}\hskip 0.6458pt\|_{\raise-1.80835pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\xi$}}\,}{\|{\mathfrak{f}}\|_{\xi}}{\|{\mathfrak{f}}\|_{\xi}}{\|{\mathfrak{f}}\|_{\xi}}\big)^{-1}\,.

Hence,
(\text{1}+{\mathfrak{f}})^{-1}\in C[[\Xi^{{\mathsf{E}}_{{\mathfrak{f}}}^{\infty}}]]_{\xi}
.
∎

Corollary B.13
.

Let
C
be a Banach algebra and let
{\mathfrak{f}}=\sum_{{\mathsf{e}}}c_{{\mathsf{e}}}\Xi^{{\mathsf{e}}}\in C[[\Xi^{{\mathsf{E}}_{+}}]]_{*}

be a convergent generalized power series such that
c_{0}
is invertible. Then the generalized power series
{\mathfrak{f}}^{-1}
, which exists according to Corollary
B.7
, is convergent.

Proof.

Let
{\mathfrak{g}}=c_{0}^{-1}{\mathfrak{f}}-\text{1}
. Then

{\mathfrak{f}}=c_{0}(\text{1}+{\mathfrak{g}})\quad\mbox{ and }0\not\in{\mathsf{E}}_{{\mathfrak{g}}}\,.

We know that
{\mathfrak{g}}(\xi)
exists for sufficiently small
\xi
, and that
{\mathfrak{g}}(\xi)\to\text{0}
in
C
as
\xi\to 0
. Hence, there exists
\xi>0
such that
\mathchoice{\|\hskip 0.6458pt{\mathfrak{g}}\hskip 0.6458pt\|_{\raise-1.80835pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\xi$}}\,}{\|{\mathfrak{g}}\|_{\xi}}{\|{\mathfrak{g}}\|_{\xi}}{\|{\mathfrak{g}}\|_{\xi}}<1
.
We can then apply Theorem
B.12
to conclude that
\text{1}+{\mathfrak{g}}
is invertible in

C[[\Xi^{{\mathsf{E}}_{+}}]]_{\xi}
. The inverse of
{\mathfrak{f}}
is given by

{\mathfrak{f}}^{-1}=(\text{1}+{\mathfrak{g}})^{-1}c_{0}^{-1}=\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\big(c_{0}^{-1}(c_{0}-{\mathfrak{f}})\big)^{k}\,c_{0}^{-1}\,.

∎
